// Pure-data marker — no virtual methods. Used only for static_cast
// disambiguation and for typed dispatch through the FInterface table.
class IDamageable {
public:
    // No member functions; this is a tag type. Real dispatch is through
    // the FClass-resident FInterface table.
};

// Per-class implementation table emitted at the implementing C# class's .cs.cpp:
// (Example: XPickup : XActor, IDamageable, IInteractable)
//
// extern "C" void XPickup_IDamageable_TakeDamage(::XObject* self, float amount) { /* body */ }
// extern "C" bool XPickup_IDamageable_get_IsAlive(::XObject* self) { /* body */ }
//
// const ::XCore::Reflect::FInterfaceImplTable XPickup_IDamageable_ImplTable = {
//     /* interfaceClass */ &IDamageable_Interface,
//     /* methods */ {
//         &XPickup_IDamageable_TakeDamage,
//         &XPickup_IDamageable_get_IsAlive,
//     }
// };
//
// The FClass for XPickup contains a TArray<FInterfaceImplTable*>; dispatch is:
//   cls->Interfaces[FindIndex(IDamageable_Interface)]->Methods[methodIdx](self, args)

Explicit interface implementation (void IDamageable.TakeDamage(...)) [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-15]. The implementation method is emitted with a mangled name that includes the interface name (e.g., _v1..._XPickup__IDamageable__TakeDamage_...) so it cannot be called via a XPickup-typed reference; only the FInterface table dispatch resolves it. Name-conflict disambiguation between two interfaces (e.g., class Foo : ISaveable, IInteractable where both define void Activate()) uses explicit implementation: each interface's Activate() emits a distinct mangled symbol.

Hot-reload of interface implementations. The FInterfaceImplTable lives in .rodata at the implementing class's .cs.cpp; replacing the class via OnClassReplaced updates the FClass's Interfaces TArray pointer to the new table. Layout-frozen mid-session (per §8.2): adding or removing an interface implementation is rejected by XLiveCoding's Phase 1 layout-drift gate (XIL2CPP145; FIX-C-HIGH-05).

C# enum EFoo with [XEnum]. Emits as C++ scoped enum class:

// C# input:
//   [XEnum]
//   public enum EFlowState : uint8_t { Closed, Open, Throttled }

// C++ output:
enum class EFlowState : uint8_t {
    Closed = 0,
    Open = 1,
    Throttled = 2
};

// FEnum descriptor emitted by XHT.

Generic class declaration. Emits as C++ template class. Closed instantiations reached by the program emit per-instantiation FClass + GC scaffolding:

// C# input:
//   public class Container<T> where T : XObject
//   {
//       public List<T> items = new();
//   }

// C++ output (Container.cs.h):
template <typename T>
class Container {
public:
    ::XCore::Container::TArray<::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<T>> items;
};

// For each closed instantiation reached (e.g., Container<XActor>), XIL2CPP emits:
//   - one FClass for the closed type
//   - one explicit template instantiation
//   - GC scaffolding (XGCRootSpan in the items field's constructor)

Critical design decision (generic instantiation walk). The closed-instantiation walk runs at Pass 3 semantic analysis with depth bound 16 and HashSet-based cycle detection. The walk is deterministic: types are visited in source-declaration order. Cross-module generic instantiations require the depending module to emit the FClass (per Constraint §2.14: “each generic instantiation is a distinct FClass with a name suffix”).

C# partial class/struct/interface. Roslyn merges partial declarations at parse time. XIL2CPP normalizes the parse tree to a single merged declaration before emit. Two or more partial declarations across files for the same type emit a single .cs.cpp file, named by the principal file (the file containing the primary constructor / the first-declared file in lexicographic order).

C# partial methods [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-14]. A partial method declaration without a body has its call sites ELIDED at AST-normalization (Pass 2): every obj.PartialMethod(args) call where the resolved target has no body is replaced with empty-statement, and the args are evaluated (preserving side effects). A partial method with a body emits normally. Mixing bodied/un-bodied across files of the same partial class is supported; the merge happens at the Roslyn semantic-model stage before XIL2CPP sees the unified declaration. Partial-method-without-body whose call expression has side effects emits warning XIL2CPP018 — call site to unbodied partial method 'M' has side-effecting arguments; arguments are evaluated but the call is elided.

C# static class [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-13]. C# static class (cannot be instantiated; only static members) emits as C++ namespace (for top-level statics) or as a C++ struct with deleted constructor (for nested statics where the C# nesting matters):

// C# input:
//   public static class XMath
//   {
//       public static float Lerp(float a, float b, float t) => a + (b - a) * t;
//   }

// C++ output:
namespace XMath {
    inline float Lerp(float a, float b, float t) noexcept { return a + (b - a) * t; }
}
// Static methods become free functions in the namespace.

Nested types [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-15]. C# Outer.Nested emits as C++ Outer::Nested (nested class), preserving the structural relationship. Multi-level nesting (Outer.Middle.Inner) emits the nesting chain. Generic-outer + nested types: the closed-instantiation walk enumerates per-outer-instantiation per-inner-instantiation combinations and emits each pair as a distinct closed C++ class.

Sealed classes (devirtualization opportunity) [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-16]. A C# sealed class Foo : Base emits a C++ class with the final keyword: class Foo final : public Base { ... };. Sealed override methods also emit final. This allows the C++ compiler to devirtualize the FClass-driven dispatcher at call sites where the static type is known to be sealed.

Method hiding (new modifier) [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-13]. C# class Derived : Base { public new void Foo() { ... } } means the derived Foo HIDES the base's Foo rather than overriding it. The static dispatch resolves to the derived form when the static type is Derived (or below in the chain) and to the base form when the static type is Base. Emit: each hidden method gets its own mangled symbol with the declaring-type as part of the mangling; the FClass virtual-method-table contains the BASE's Foo slot pointing at Base::Foo; Derived's Foo is a peer free function with a different mangled name. Call sites that are statically typed to Derived resolve to Derived::Foo; call sites statically typed to Base go through the FClass dispatcher and reach Base::Foo.

5.2 Method-level mappings (Tier 1 vs Tier 2)

Every C# method emits a C++ free function with an explicit self parameter per Contract §2.3:

// C# input:
//   public void Valve.SetOpenFraction(float inFraction) { ... }

// C++ output (Tier 2 form; method classified as Tier 2 by Pass 4):
extern "C" void _v1ab12cd34__Simgenics__XPact__GameFramework__Valve__SetOpenFraction_P_R_Simgenics__XPact__GameFramework__Valve_V_float(
    ::Simgenics::XPact::GameFramework::Valve* self,
    float inFraction) noexcept
{
    // Translated body. No exception handling. No XResult.
    // FStackMapRecord emitted to .rodata; safe-point check at entry.
    XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    self->openFraction = inFraction;
    self->Recompute();
}

// Per-function FStackMapRecord in .rodata:
static const ::XCore::Reflect::FStackMapRecord
_StackMap__v1ab12cd34__Valve__SetOpenFraction = {
    /* pcRangeBegin */ 0,
    /* pcRangeEnd   */ 0xFFFFFFFFu,
    /* numLiveRefs  */ 1,  // self
    /* _pad         */ 0,
    /* liveRefOffsets[] */ { 0 }  // self is at argument-register; offset 0
};

// And:
//
// C++ output (Tier 1 form; method classified as Tier 1 by Pass 4, e.g., because
// of an exported entry, [CanThrow] attribute, or unproven cross-module callee):

extern "C" void _v1ab12cd34__Simgenics__XPact__GameFramework__Valve__SetOpenFraction_Shim_P_R_..._O_XResult(
    ::Simgenics::XPact::GameFramework::Valve* self,
    float inFraction,
    ::XCore::Exception::XResult* outResult)
{
    // Dev/Test lowering (native exceptions):
    try {
        _v1ab12cd34__..._SetOpenFraction_Body(self, inFraction);
        outResult->discriminator = ::XCore::Exception::XResult::Discriminator::Success;
        // void return: no value to construct in outResult->success.buffer
    }
    catch (const ::XCore::Exception::XCSharpException& ex) {
        outResult->discriminator = ::XCore::Exception::XResult::Discriminator::Error;
        outResult->error.code = ex.code;
        outResult->error.typeName = ex.typeName;
        outResult->error.message = ex.message;
        outResult->error.stackTrace = ex.captureStack();
    }
}

// In Shipping lowering, the shim body is identical except the try/catch is removed
// (per Contract §5.7) and the body writes outResult directly.

Mangling rule. Per Contract §2.2 line 480-492, the mangled name is:

{ContractVersion}__{Namespace}::{Type}::{Method}{GenericArgManglings}{ParamManglings}

where components are length-prefixed per the Itanium ABI convention. The linker-visible form replaces :: with __, drops parentheses, and replaces commas / spaces with _. The walk is deterministic; XIL2CPP's internal name-dict is sorted by name before emit.

Mangling participates in / excludes from [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-01 / FIX-C-MED-02 / FIX-C-MED-03]. The mangled name depends only on:

  1. The Contract version hash (e.g., 13.10+bbcc0292b75e9a10).
  2. The namespace-qualified type name.
  3. The method name (CLR convention).
  4. Generic-arg manglings (in source-declaration order).
  5. Parameter manglings (in declaration order), with discriminators per Contract §2.2 line 491: R reference, V value, B by-ref, I in, O out. Forward-commit Contract Rev 14 (per FIX-B-HIGH-03 / FIX-C-HIGH-02): add K for ref readonly, Q for nullability annotations on reference parameter types.

The mangled name explicitly does NOT depend on:

  • Method body content (statements, expressions, local variables, called functions). This is load-bearing for hot-reload: editing the body of a Tier 1 function must produce the same mangled symbol so XLiveCoding's RVA-rewrite trampoline can find the patch target.
  • Compile-time-only attributes ([Obsolete], [Conditional], [CallerMemberName], [CallerFilePath], [CallerLineNumber], [Experimental]; all attributes inheriting System.Attribute that are NOT flagged with the [XAttribute] meta-attribute). These are documentation-only changes; adding or removing them must not break hot-reload.
  • File path (the absolute or repo-relative path of the C# source file). This decouples symbol identity from filesystem layout; moving a .cs file across folders does not change mangling, and two developers with different repo paths produce byte-identical symbols (provided their Roslyn / .NET SDK versions match).

Linker-symbol separator hygiene [Rev 2: FIX-DEFERRED-Rev3 per FIX-B-HIGH-25]. The current Rev 2 linker-visible separator is __; this is reserved by the C/C++ standards for the implementation, so there is a theoretical collision risk with platform-reserved symbols. Forward-commit Contract Rev 14 to introduce a non-reserved separator (e.g., _o_ — "outer separator"). Risk acknowledged in §14 audit table; not blocking for Rev 2.

Virtual method dispatch. C# virtual methods emit a dispatcher free function:

// C# input:
//   public class Actor : XObject { public virtual void Tick(float dt) { ... } }
//   public class Pickup : Actor { public override void Tick(float dt) { ... } }

// C++ output:
// Per-method dispatcher (free function) for Actor::Tick:
extern "C" void _v1..._Actor__Tick_Dispatcher_P_R_Actor_V_float(
    ::Actor* self,
    float dt) noexcept
{
    XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    // FClass*-driven vtable lookup; the FClass exposes a per-virtual-method slot table.
    const ::XCore::Reflect::FClass* cls = self->GetClass();
    using ImplFn = void(*)(::Actor*, float);
    ImplFn impl = reinterpret_cast<ImplFn>(cls->VirtualMethods[ActorTickSlotIndex]);
    impl(self, dt);  // tail-call to resolved free function
}

// Per-concrete-type body (free function); each derived class emits one:
extern "C" void _v1..._Pickup__Tick_P_R_Pickup_V_float(
    ::Pickup* self,
    float dt) noexcept
{
    XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    // Translated C# body of Pickup.Tick.
}

Critical design decision (vtable dispatch). XPact uses an FClass-driven dispatch (per the FakeVTable pattern) rather than a C++ vtable. Rationale per XCoreXObject.html §1.3: avoid virtuals on the GC-relevant surface so hot-reload class replacement can swap function-pointer slots without ABI breaks. The dispatcher's overhead is one extra load (the slot index lookup) compared to a C++ vtable; the gain is hot-reload safety.

Static method. Emits as a free function with no self parameter:

extern "C" float _v1..._Simgenics__XPact__Math__Lerp_P_V_float_V_float_V_float(
    float a, float b, float t) noexcept
{
    XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    return a + (b - a) * t;
}

Constructor (.ctor). Method name $ctor:

extern "C" void _v1..._Foo__$ctor_P_R_Foo_V_int(::Foo* self, int32_t initialValue) noexcept
{
    XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    self->value = initialValue;
}

Static constructor (.cctor). Method name $cctor:

// Emitted in an anonymous-namespace static initializer block:
namespace {
    struct _Foo_StaticCtorInit {
        _Foo_StaticCtorInit() {
            _v1..._Foo__$cctor_P_();
        }
    };
    static _Foo_StaticCtorInit _Foo_StaticCtorInit_Instance;
}

Operator overloads. CLR-convention method names:

// C# input:
//   public static FPoint operator +(FPoint a, FPoint b) => new FPoint(a.X + b.X, a.Y + b.Y);

// C++ output:
extern "C" ::FPoint _v1..._FPoint__op_Add_P_V_FPoint_V_FPoint(::FPoint a, ::FPoint b) noexcept
{
    return ::FPoint{a.X + b.X, a.Y + b.Y};
}

// And a C++-side operator+ defined inline in the .cs.h for ergonomic call sites:
inline ::FPoint operator+(::FPoint a, ::FPoint b) {
    return _v1..._FPoint__op_Add_P_V_FPoint_V_FPoint(a, b);
}

Property getter/setter pair. Emits as two free functions:

// C# input:
//   public int Foo { get { ... } set { ... } }

// C++ output (Tier 2 form):
extern "C" int32_t _v1..._Foo__get_Foo_P_R_Foo(::Foo* self) noexcept { return self->_FooBackingField; }
extern "C" void    _v1..._Foo__set_Foo_P_R_Foo_V_int(::Foo* self, int32_t value) noexcept {
    self->_FooBackingField = value;
}

If the property is on an XObject-derived type and the property's type is itself XObject-derived (e.g., public XActor Owner { get; set; }), the setter emits XPACT_GC_STORE per §6. Explicit emit example [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-23]:

// C# input:
//   public XActor Owner { get; set; }   // on an XObject-derived type Foo
//
// C++ output:
extern "C" ::XActor* _v1..._Foo__get_Owner_P_R_Foo(::Foo* self) noexcept {
    return self->_OwnerBackingField;
}
extern "C" void _v1..._Foo__set_Owner_P_R_Foo_V_XActorPtr(::Foo* self, ::XActor* value) noexcept {
    XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    XPACT_GC_STORE(self, &self->_OwnerBackingField, value);
}

ref / out parameter emit [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-CRIT-3]. C# ref T / out T for value types and non-XObject reference types emit as C++ T& (ref) or T* (out, with required write before return). For XObject reference types — ref XActor or out XActor — BANNED in MVP with diagnostic XIL2CPP005 — ref/out parameter of XObject-reference type is not supported in MVP; return XActor or use the Result<T, E> pattern instead. Rationale: the callee writing through the parameter cannot know the correct parent_obj argument for the XPACT_GC_STORE(parent_obj, slot, newValue) write barrier (the parent is per-caller). Banning eliminates the silent loss of references through cards. Post-MVP: an XRef<T> wrapper-type protocol may be introduced (value-type pair {XObject* parent, XPtr<T>* slot}) that the caller constructs at the call site; out of scope for MVP.

Extension methods [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-11]. C# public static R Ext.Method(this T self, A a) { ... } in a static class emits as a free function with self as the first arg (the same shape as a regular instance method). Call sites obj.Method(a) are resolved at Pass 1 (semantic model) to Ext::Method(obj, a) and emit as free-function calls.

Operator overloads — complete CLR list [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MINOR-02]. The full set of CLR operator method names XIL2CPP recognizes: op_Implicit, op_Explicit, op_Addition, op_Subtraction, op_Multiply, op_Division, op_Modulus, op_BitwiseAnd, op_BitwiseOr, op_ExclusiveOr, op_LeftShift, op_RightShift, op_UnsignedRightShift (C# 11), op_Equality, op_Inequality, op_GreaterThan, op_LessThan, op_GreaterThanOrEqual, op_LessThanOrEqual, op_Increment, op_Decrement, op_True, op_False, op_OnesComplement, op_LogicalNot, op_UnaryPlus, op_UnaryNegation; checked variants (op_CheckedAddition, etc.) per C# 11. All emit as free functions; the binding to C++ operator is per-target.

User-defined conversions (implicit / explicit) [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-08]. public static implicit operator U(T x) emits as a free function op_Implicit_T_to_U AND a non-explicit C++ constructor U::U(T x) (or T::operator U() const member if U is XObject-derived and cannot be modified). public static explicit operator U(T x) emits the same shape but the C++ constructor is explicit.

Constructor chaining [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-09]. C# : this(args) emits a free-function tail-call to the other ctor variant. C# : base(args): for non-XObject base, emits as direct call to base ctor at the start of the derived ctor body; for XObject-derived base, the : base(args) args are NOT passed through NewObject<T> (which takes only outer/name/flags) — instead, the args are routed to a Z_PostInitProperties_<Type> user-init slot per the XObject lifecycle pattern, OR the constructor declaration is rejected at parse time (Pass 3) with diagnostic XIL2CPP097 — XObject-derived constructor with explicit base(args) is not supported; use Initialize(...) method or override Z_PostInitProperties.

5.3 Statement and expression mappings

Local variable declarations. var x = expr → auto x = expr; (with explicit type if auto can't deduce). int x = 5; → int32_t x = 5;. Locals that are XObject* are tracked in the Pass 6 stack-map record.

Assignments.

// C# input (non-reference LHS):
//   x = 5;

// C++ output:
x = 5;

// C# input (XObject-reference LHS):
//   actor.Owner = newOwner;

// C++ output:
XPACT_GC_STORE(actor, &actor->Owner, newOwner);

The XPACT_GC_STORE macro is defined in XCoreXObject/XGCWriteBarrier.h and lowers to:

#define XPACT_GC_STORE(parent_obj, slot_ptr, new_value) \
    do { \
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_WriteBarrier( \
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(slot_ptr), \
            static_cast<void*>(new_value)); \
        *(slot_ptr) = (new_value); \
    } while (0)

Critical design decision (XPACT_GC_STORE emission). The barrier is emitted at every reference-storing assignment, including: direct field writes (obj.Field = x), indirect field writes through pointer chains (a.b.c.Field = x emits a barrier at the final store; intermediate reads are bare loads), array slot writes (arr[i] = x where arr is TArray<XPtr<T>>), TMap slot writes via map[k] = v, and aggregate-assignment via record with-expressions when the record contains XObject refs. Per Constraint §2.6, the barrier is recognized by type analysis: any C# field whose reflected type is FObjectProperty, FWeakObjectProperty, or FStructProperty containing a reference / FArrayProperty containing a reference / FMapProperty containing a reference / FSetProperty containing a reference is a barrier target. The codegen pass inserts the macro expansion at the AST-lowering stage.

Conditional expressions. cond ? a : b → cond ? a : b. Null-coalescing a ?? b → (a != nullptr ? a : b). Null-conditional a?.b → (a != nullptr ? a->b : nullptr).

default(T) expression [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-03 / FIX-A-MED-25]. Emits per-T:

  • default(int) / default(float) / default(value_type T) → T{} (C++ value-initialization — 0 for ints, 0.0f for floats, zero-init for structs).
  • default(XActor) / any XObject-derived T → static_cast<T*>(nullptr).
  • default(XPtr<T>) → XPtr<T>{} (default-constructed empty XPtr; null-pointer state).
  • default(struct_with_XObject_field T) → T{} (value-init; embedded XObject* is nullptr; stack-map MUST still register the slot when stored on the stack — see §6.1).

Target-typed bare default (C# 7.1+) inherits the target type from context; same emit per the resolved type.

is null vs == null [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-05]. These are NOT equivalent:

  • expr is null emits a bare pointer comparison (or XOptional<T>::HasValue() == false for nullable value types). Bypasses any user-defined operator==.
  • expr == null emits a call to the user-defined operator== if one exists; falls back to pointer comparison otherwise.

For ref types with user-defined operator== that does field-by-field equality, obj == null could dereference a null arg; obj is null is safe. Use is null for null checks against XObject-derived types.

Multi-step reference write (obj.a.b.c.Field = x) [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-15]. The chain lowers to a sequence of Raw()-promoted reads on each intermediate XPtr followed by the final assignment with barrier:

// C# input:
//   outer.mid.inner.actor = newActor;

// C++ output (if mid + inner are XPtr<T> fields):
::Inner* _tmp_inner = outer.mid.Raw()->inner.Raw();
XPACT_GC_STORE(_tmp_inner, &_tmp_inner->actor, newActor);

XPtr's Raw() promotion returns a stable T* (NOT a by-value temporary that dies at end-of-statement). The barrier's parent_obj is the directly-enclosing object (_tmp_inner), not the chain root. If any intermediate XPtr returns by-value temporary instead of stable Raw() promotion, Pass 3 rejects the chain with diagnostic XIL2CPP063 — Chained write through XPtr<T> intermediate is undefined; assign to a stable local first.

out var x declaration pattern [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MINOR-03]. bool ok = dict.TryGetValue(k, out var v); declares v at the call site's enclosing scope (or smaller if pattern-narrowed); the variable is forward-declared if needed in C++. Same emit as the equivalent T v; bool ok = dict.TryGetValue(k, out v);.

Discard _ semantics [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MINOR-05]. The _ token in tuple deconstruction (var (a, _) = t), is-patterns (obj is (_, var b)), out-args (Foo(out _)), and lambdas ((_, _) => 0) is a discard. No local is emitted; the value is dropped. Pass 2 normalization replaces _ uses with explicit "ignore" placeholders.

goto case / goto default [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-36]. Only legal inside a switch STATEMENT (not switch expression). Maps to C++ goto _case_X; where _case_X is a synthesized label inserted at the case body's start. goto inside async / iterator methods is rejected at Roslyn parse time (CS0159 etc.); XIL2CPP relies on Roslyn rejection.

Loops.

// C# for loop:
//   for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) { body }

// C++ output:
for (int32_t i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    XPACT_BACKEDGE_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();  // emitted iff n > STATIC_LOOP_THRESHOLD (default 16 iter or undecidable)
    body
}

// C# foreach over List<T>:
//   foreach (var item in list) { body }

// C++ output:
for (auto& item : list.AsRangeView()) {
    XPACT_BACKEDGE_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    body
}

// C# foreach over HashSet<T> on sim-path:
//   emits XIL2CPP041 warning — non-det iteration order; cross-arch behavior may differ.
//   author can suppress with [XFunction(SimPathHashSetIterOK = true)] on the enclosing method.

Collection expressions. [1, 2, 3] targeted at List<int>:

::XCore::Container::TArray<int32_t> tmp;
tmp.Reserve(3);
tmp.Add(1); tmp.Add(2); tmp.Add(3);
// tmp is the result.

[1, 2, 3] targeted at int[]: int32_t tmp[3] = {1, 2, 3};.

[1, 2, 3] targeted at ReadOnlySpan<int>:

int32_t _stack_arr[3] = {1, 2, 3};
::XCore::Span::TReadOnlySpan<int32_t> tmp(_stack_arr, 3);

Object creation (new Foo()) for non-XObject. Same as C++ new Foo() (stack-construct for value types; std::shared_ptr-managed if reference-identity-observed).

Object creation for XObject-derived (XObject.New<Foo>(outer, name, flags)). Maps to NewObject<XFoo>(outer, name, flags):

// C# input:
//   var pickup = XObject.New<HealthPickup>(outer: this, name: "Pickup_1", flags: RF_NoFlags);

// C++ output (non-sim-path):
auto* pickup = ::XCore::Reflect::NewObject<XHealthPickup>(this, ::FName("Pickup_1"), RF_NoFlags);

// C++ output (sim-path TU):
XPACT_CHECK_SL(::XCore::HAL::IsSimPathThread() || !::XCore::HAL::IsSimPathTU(),
               "sim-path NewObject called from non-SimPathSerialExecutor thread");
auto* pickup = ::XCore::Reflect::NewObject<XHealthPickup>(this, ::FName("Pickup_1"), RF_NoFlags);

Object creation for XObject-derived via bare new — COMPILE-TIME ERROR. Per Locked Commitment 3: emits XIL2CPP001 at Pass 3.

5.4 Field and property emit

Every C# field (and every auto-property's backing field) emits as a C++ member in source-declaration order. The reflection metadata (FProperty descriptor) is emitted by XHT; XIL2CPP emits only the storage and accessor bodies.

Required members. required int X { get; set; } emits:

  • A C++ member int32_t X = 0;.
  • FProperty descriptor (XHT side) carries the CPF_RequiredInit bit (per XCore-4b.html line 726).
  • Constructor body emits a runtime check: if the C# object initializer did not set X, the construction aborts. The C# compile-error path catches most cases at parse time; the runtime check is belt-and-suspenders for deserialization paths where the initializer is bypassed.

Init-only properties [Rev 2: rewrite per FIX-A-MED-24 / FIX-B-MEDIUM-12]. public int X { get; init; } emits the setter as an ordinary free function with the same body shape as a regular setter. C# init accessor restrictions are enforced by Roslyn at parse time (CS8852 for non-init-context calls); by the time XIL2CPP runs (Pass 3), all violations have already been rejected. XIL2CPP does NOT emit a mangling-suffix-based callability discriminator (the Rev 1 design proposed this but it was over-engineered — the language already enforces). The init-setter emit is byte-identical to a regular set-setter emit; the language semantics are the gate.

readonly fields [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-08]. readonly T field; can be assigned in declaration or in constructor only. Per type:

  • readonly int x; → C++ int const x; (const value member; init in ctor mem-init-list).
  • readonly XActor owner; → C++ ::XActor* const owner; (const POINTER, mutable pointee — matches C# semantics: the reference cannot be reassigned but owner->position = ... is fine).
  • readonly XPtr<XActor> owner; → C++ ::XPtr<::XActor> const owner; (const XPtr; same).

Roslyn enforces "no post-ctor assignment" at parse time via CS0191; XIL2CPP relies on that gate.

volatile field keyword [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-12]. C# volatile T field; maps to C++ std::atomic<T> field; (NOT C++ volatile — C++ volatile has WEAKER semantics than C#'s and would be silently wrong). All reads emit field.load(std::memory_order_acquire); all writes emit field.store(value, std::memory_order_release). Layout caveat: std::atomic<T> may have different alignment than T on ARM64; if alignment differs from natural T, emit diagnostic XIL2CPP019 — volatile field type 'T' has incompatible alignment for std::atomic on target platform; consider Interlocked APIs or a different design.

Auto-property backing field naming [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-09]. Canonical XPact name: __BackingField_<X> (deterministic, C-identifier-safe, no angle brackets). Both XHT (for FProperty descriptor emit) and XIL2CPP (for storage emit) MUST use this exact form. A drift between the two emits diagnostic XIL2CPP145 — backing-field naming mismatch between XHT and XIL2CPP for property '<X>' (XHT emits '<name1>', XIL2CPP emits '<name2>'). Documented in §10.5 (shared binding table).

Static fields holding XObject references [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-HIGH-6]. A C# static field of XObject-derived type (e.g., public static XGameInstance Singleton) emits:

  • The field as an XPtr<T> storage in the .cs.cpp anonymous namespace.
  • A static-init block that registers the slot via XGCRoot::AddRoot at module load.
  • A setter implementation that drives the AddRoot/RemoveRoot lifecycle: 
    // C# input:
//   public static XGameInstance Singleton;
namespace { ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XGameInstance> XEngine_Singleton; }

extern "C" void _v1..._XEngine__set_Singleton_V_XGameInstancePtr(::XGameInstance* newValue) noexcept {
    XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
    // SATB barrier (capture old value before reassignment):
    ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_WriteBarrier(
        reinterpret_cast<void**>(&XEngine_Singleton), newValue);
    if (XEngine_Singleton.Raw()) ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRoot::RemoveRoot(XEngine_Singleton.Raw());
    XEngine_Singleton = ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XGameInstance>(newValue);
    if (newValue) ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRoot::AddRoot(newValue);
}

// Per-module load:
//   static_init block: if XEngine_Singleton is set at decl-init, AddRoot fires.
// Per-module unload:
//   static-dtor block: if XEngine_Singleton is set, RemoveRoot fires (releases the reference;
//   the XObject becomes GC-collectable on next sweep).

[ThreadStatic] static fields are BANNED on sim-path with diagnostic XIL2CPP057 — [ThreadStatic] is banned on sim-path TUs (single SerialExecutor; semantics collapse) [Rev 4: applied FIX-F-MED-2]. On non-sim-path, each thread that the gameplay-tier runtime creates registers the slot at thread-create and unregisters at thread-exit via the XPact task-system entry/exit hooks.

Primary-constructor capture semantics [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-01]. Two distinct emit shapes:

  • Record primary ctors — public record Foo(int X, int Y); — parameters become public init-only FProperty entries (reflectable, serializable, replicable).
  • Class primary ctors — public class Foo(int x) { public int Sum(int y) => x + y; } — parameters are private mutable capture fields (NOT reflectable, NOT serializable, NOT replicable). They are NOT FProperty entries; emit as private C++ fields named __PrimaryCapture_<x>.

If a class is [XClass]-attributed and a primary-ctor parameter is referenced in any method body (a capture), emit warning XIL2CPP017 — class primary-constructor parameter '<n>' captured into method body; XClass-reflectable types should use explicit fields for clarity.

[ModuleInitializer] attribute (C# 9+) [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-24]. A static method carrying [ModuleInitializer] emits as an anonymous-namespace static-initializer function that calls the method at DLL load time. Runs before any user code.

[Conditional("X")] attribute [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-25]. A method carrying [Conditional("DEBUG")] has its call sites elided when the module's conditional_symbols manifest field does not contain "DEBUG". Implemented at Pass 2 normalization: walk every invocation; if the resolved method is [Conditional("X")] and X is not in the conditional symbols, replace the call with the empty statement (evaluating any side-effecting args is dropped — matching Roslyn's behavior, which evaluates args at the call site only if they would have an effect on observable state at compile time).

[StructLayout(Pack = N)] attribute [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-18]. Banned on ARM64 targets when N < natural alignment of fields: emit diagnostic XIL2CPP093 — Packed struct on ARM64 requires unaligned access; pack-attribute is BANNED for non-1-byte fields on ARM64. Build exit 41. Rationale: ARM64 traps unaligned access for many instructions; the conservative choice is to reject at compile time rather than ship a perf regression.

Record with-expression emit [Rev 3 per FIX-D-MED-13]. C# 9+ with-expression on records invokes the synthesized <Clone>$() method (the record's clone-then-init pattern). XIL2CPP lowers p with { X = 10 } as:

// C# input:
//   public record Point(int X, int Y);
//   var p1 = new Point(5, 7);
//   var p2 = p1 with { X = 10 };

// C++ output:
auto p2 = p1.Clone();       // p1's synthesized <Clone>$() emits a copy of the record
p2.X = 10;                  // init-setter via privileged path (the with-expression-context bypass)
                            // p2.Y carries p1.Y == 7 unchanged

The init-accessor's parse-time restriction (CS8852: "init-only property can only be assigned in object initializer or constructor") is relaxed inside the with-expression context: the lowering's p2.X = 10 is privileged by Roslyn at AST normalization, so the XIL2CPP emit treats it as a legal setter call. For record-class (not record-struct), Clone() is a virtual member that returns a heap-allocated copy via std::shared_ptr; for record-struct, Clone() returns a stack-resident copy by value.

Nullable reference types. XActor? Owner emits the same C++ member as XActor Owner (no extra storage); the FProperty descriptor (XHT side) carries the CPF_NullableReferenceType bit.

Nullable value types. int? emits as XOptional<int32_t> (a 5-byte struct: 4 bytes int + 1 byte has-value; aligned to 8 bytes net).

5.5 String emit

C# string is mapped to the XCSharpString 8-byte value-type handle per Contract §6.1 line 1098-1104:

struct XCSharpString {
    const ::XCore::Container::FString* m_storage;
};

String literals. Every C# string literal in the source emits as a constinit const FString in the binary's .rodata section. The compile-time interning rule per XCore-4a.html line 1432-1437: two distinct C# literals with the same UTF-8 content resolve to the same address. A build-time error fires if two distinct C# literals would resolve to the same address but have different content (impossible by the interning rule; the error is a safety net).

// C# input:
//   var s = "hello";
//   var t = nameof(SomeMethod);
//   var u = "SomeMethod";  // assume SomeMethod is the method name

// C++ output (in .rodata):
constinit const ::XCore::Container::FString _String_hello("hello", 5);
constinit const ::XCore::Container::FString _String_SomeMethod("SomeMethod", 10);

// At use site:
::XCSharpString s{&_String_hello};
::XCSharpString t{&_String_SomeMethod};
::XCSharpString u{&_String_SomeMethod};   // SAME ADDRESS as t per Contract §6.2 line 1117-1120

Pointer-identity guarantee. ReferenceEquals(nameof(X), "X") holds. The test Tests/IL2CPP/StringInternIdentity validates this across 1000+ literal pairs across two DLLs (per XCore-4a.html line 1436).

String interpolation [Rev 2: rewrite per FIX-B-HIGH-24]. $"hello {name}" lowers per C# 10's DefaultInterpolatedStringHandler pattern with a stack-allocated handler. The Rev 1 design (heap-allocated XCSharpStringBuilder) was rejected because: (a) C# 10's actual lowering does NOT use the builder pattern; (b) heap allocation per interpolation site is unacceptable on sim-path (every log line would alloc).

// C# input:
//   var s = $"hello {name}";

// AST-normalized form (matching C# 10 lowering):
//   var __h = new XDefaultInterpolatedStringHandler(literalLength: 6, formattedCount: 1);
//   __h.AppendLiteral("hello ");
//   __h.AppendFormatted(name);
//   var s = __h.ToStringAndClear();

// C++ output:
::XCore::Container::XDefaultInterpolatedStringHandler __h(/*lit*/ 6, /*fmt*/ 1);
__h.AppendLiteral(::XCSharpString{&_String_hello_space});
__h.AppendFormatted(name);
auto s = __h.ToStringAndClear();
// __h is stack-allocated; its internal buffer is stack-storage up to a sim-path threshold
// (default 256 bytes); overflow allocates heap via XPact.Sim attribution.

XDefaultInterpolatedStringHandler is part of the XPact.CSharp.BCL NuGet (carry-forward to XCore-4a Rev N if not already present). On sim-path, the handler stays stack-resident; if interpolation overflows the stack buffer, build emits warning XIL2CPP084 — sim-path interpolation overflows stack buffer; consider shorter interpolations or manual concatenation.

System.String utility methods [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-29]. The mapping table augments:

  • String.IsNullOrEmpty(s) → XCSharpString::IsNullOrEmpty(s).
  • String.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(s) → XCSharpString::IsNullOrWhiteSpace(s).
  • String.Concat(a, b, ...) → XCSharpString::Concat(a, b, ...).
  • String.Join(sep, parts) → XCSharpString::Join(sep, parts).

String.Equals ordinal semantics [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-28]. XCSharpString::operator== emits as pointer-equality on m_storage — sufficient given the literal-interning invariant (per Contract §6.2 line 1117-1120). Non-literal string construction (e.g., from XDefaultInterpolatedStringHandler::ToStringAndClear) also routes through the same intern table, so == always equals pointer-equality. C# == on string is also ordinal (not culture-sensitive) per C# spec; this matches.

C# char mapping [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-23]. C# char is a 16-bit UTF-16 code unit; emits as C++ char16_t (NOT wchar_t which is 32-bit on Linux/Android Quest 3 and would silently break cross-arch). Operations like char.IsDigit(c) map to XPact helpers (XCharOps::IsDigit) that operate on char16_t and do not depend on the platform's wchar_t model.

FName from literal vs runtime string [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-2]. C# string literals used to construct FName at the source (e.g., XObject.New<T>(outer, name: "Pickup_1", ...)) emit at compile time as _FName_Pickup_1 — a constexpr 8-byte handle whose Index is computed at compile time via FName's XXH3 scalar hash (deterministic; matches the byte-identical intern table). Runtime-constructed FNames (e.g., FName(someRuntimeString)) emit ::FName(someRuntimeString) and ARE subject to the first-use-wins interning ordering. Sim-path TUs constructing FName from a non-literal string emit diagnostic XIL2CPP049 — Sim-path code constructs FName from a non-literal string; this is non-deterministic across arches due to interning ordering. Use a compile-time string literal.

nameof() semantics [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-04 / FIX-A-MED-10]. Per C# 11/12 spec, nameof(X) resolves at the source-of-expression to the literal name as it appears in the source. Specifically:

  • nameof(SomeMethod) → literal "SomeMethod".
  • nameof(this.SomeProp) (C# 12 instance-member nameof inside attribute args) → literal "SomeProp".
  • nameof(T) for a generic type parameter → literal "T" — the type-parameter's declared name, NOT the closed-instantiation name. Even at MyActor<XPlayer>'s closed instantiation, nameof(T) still resolves to "T".

The interned FString lookup emits the const FString* by literal; the same literal in any TU shares the interned address.

XCSharpStringBuilder [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-LOW-7; Rev 3 resolution per FIX-D-MED-11]. Rev 3 commits to the BCL Rev 2 forward-commit BCL-Rev2-STRINGBUILDER (see §14.0): the BCL surface emits using XCSharpStringBuilder = ::XCore::String::FStringBuilder; as a type alias to XCore-4a's existing FStringBuilder if its API surface covers the C# StringBuilder contract (Append, Append(char), AppendLine, Length, Clear, ToString, etc.); if a coverage gap surfaces during BCL Rev 2 implementation, XCSharpStringBuilder ships as a wrapper struct that delegates to FStringBuilder for the covered surface and provides additional methods for the uncovered surface. The alias-vs-wrapper decision is BCL Rev 2's; XIL2CPP MVP emits the name XCSharpStringBuilder uniformly. Fallback if BCL Rev 2 misses: see XIL2CPP183 (use FStringBuilder directly).

Sim-path s[i] ban. Per Contract §6.4 line 1128: emit time error XIL2CPP042 — sim-path string index 's[i]' is non-deterministic at the UTF-8 byte boundary; use s.AsSpan()[i] for byte access or s.AsCodepoints()[i] for codepoint access. Suppressible only via [XFunction(SimPathStringIndexOK = true)] on the enclosing method.

Iterator surface. s.AsSpan(), s.IndexOf(c), s.Substring(i, n), s.AsCodepoints() are mapped to XCSharpString member functions per Contract §6.4 line 1132-1141.

5.6 Pattern matching

Pattern matching at AST normalization lowers to nested if/switch chains. Examples:

// C# input:
//   string Describe(object o) => o switch {
//       int n when n < 0 => "negative",
//       int n => "positive",
//       string s => $"string of length {s.Length}",
//       _ => "unknown"
//   };

// C++ output (after AST normalization):
::XCSharpString Describe(::Object* o) {
    if (auto* p = ::XCore::Reflect::Cast<::Int32Box>(o)) {
        int32_t n = p->Value;
        if (n < 0) return ::XCSharpString{&_String_negative};
        return ::XCSharpString{&_String_positive};
    }
    if (auto* p = ::XCore::Reflect::Cast<::XCSharpString>(o)) {
        ::XCSharpString s = *p;
        // interpolated string emit per §5.5
        ...
    }
    return ::XCSharpString{&_String_unknown};
}

Note that int n as an object pattern requires boxing on the C# side. XIL2CPP MVP rejects polymorphic boxing of value types via object; per Locked Commitment 1 there's no managed boxing. Sim-path code that switches on object with value-type patterns is a build-time error XIL2CPP060 — pattern match on object with value-type pattern requires boxing; not supported on sim-path. [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-CRIT-02 / FIX-A-MED-9] Non-sim-path code is ALSO banned in MVP from implicit boxing: XIL2CPP062 — Implicit boxing of value type to object is not supported in MVP. Use a typed discriminant union (record) or an explicit Int32Box-style wrapper from XPact.CSharp.BCL.

Boxing wrappers are [XValueClass] non-XObject types — NOT XObject-derived [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-CRIT-03 — structural fix]. The Rev 2 description ("XIL2CPP-emitted boxing wrappers are reference-typed XObject-derived classes that register their boxed value as a stack/heap root") was structurally broken: any XObject-derived type triggers XIL2CPP001 at new Int32Box(5), and the call site cannot synthesize a meaningful Outer for an inline boxing expression. Per Prime Directive, Rev 3 picks the architecturally correct resolution:

  • Int32Box, FloatBox, etc. are [XValueClass]-attributed plain reference types, NOT XObject-derived. They emit per the §5.1 plain-class default (Rev 2's std::shared_ptr<T> behavior overridden by [XValueClass] to value-typed C++ structs). They have NO FClass, NO FXObjectLifecycleTable, NO 56-byte XObject header.
  • Construction path: var box = new Int32Box(5); is legal (no XIL2CPP001 fires because the type is NOT XObject-derived) and emits as a stack-resident or shared_ptr-owned C++ value-typed struct: Int32Box _box{5} or auto _box = std::make_shared<Int32Box>(5) depending on the C# storage location.
  • Hash + Equals defer to the primitive's value semantics (so Int32Box(5).Equals(Int32Box(5)) is true; Int32Box(5).GetHashCode() returns the boxed int's hash).
  • GC integration: the wrapped value is a primitive (int32_t, float, etc.) with no XObject references inside, so there's no XGCRootSpan registration needed at all — the wrapper is transparent to the collector. If a wrapper needs to hold an XObject reference (e.g., a future XObjectRefBox for an inverse use case), it would not be a boxing wrapper but a strongly-typed reference holder per the normal §6.7 struct-with-XObject-field rules.
  • Pattern-match cast: the §5.6 emit ::XCore::Reflect::Cast<::Int32Box>(o) becomes a runtime-type-check against the [XValueClass]-tagged discriminant; the implementation is a per-wrapper-type ID comparison, not an FClass lookup (because Int32Box has no FClass). The object o parameter is a discriminated-union surface that can only be produced via an explicit author wrap (object o = Int32Box.From(5);) — implicit boxing (object o = 5;) remains XIL2CPP062-banned.

Post-MVP, the wrapper set may be extended via BCL-Rev2-BOXING forward-commit to cover the full primitive enumeration; Rev 3 commits to the type-shape rule ([XValueClass] non-XObject) so the construction story is solid before BCL Rev 2 ships. Fallback if BCL Rev 2 misses the wrappers: XIL2CPP MVP can ship without the wrappers because implicit boxing is already banned (XIL2CPP062 fires before the wrapper would be invoked); authors who need explicit boxing on non-sim-path TUs receive XIL2CPP187 — Int32Box/FloatBox not yet shipped in XPact.CSharp.BCL; deferred to BCL Rev 2; consider using a typed record discriminant instead (warning, not error). MVP critical path is not blocked.

List<object> sim-path cascade [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-CRIT-03 cross-link]. Per §4.1 line 788, List<object> with value-type entries is XIL2CPP073-banned on sim-path. The non-sim-path emit per §5.7 (TArray<void*> Conservative XGCRootSpan) accepts only reference-typed entries (XObject references or [XValueClass] wrappers); a value-type entry that would trigger implicit boxing still emits XIL2CPP062 at the Add call site. Authors who explicitly construct Int32Box wrappers and add them are legal (the wrapper IS reference-typed in the [XValueClass] non-XObject sense; the Conservative XGCRootSpan correctly treats the wrapper as a non-XObject pointer and skips it in GC traversal). The TArray<void*> Conservative XGCRootSpan is therefore correct: it scans for XObject pointers and treats [XValueClass] wrapper pointers as non-matching values (the conservative four-gate check rejects them because they are not in the FXObjectArray index).

Per-pattern emit rules [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-06]. Pattern matching cascades at AST normalization into nested if/switch chains per pattern kind:

  • Constant pattern (obj is 5) → obj == 5.
  • Type pattern (obj is SomeType x) → (::XCore::Reflect::Cast<SomeType>(obj) != nullptr) + assignment of the cast result to a new local x.
  • Property pattern (obj is { X: > 0, Y: 5 }) → null-check on obj (if ref type), then per-property getter call + per-property predicate. For an XObject property where the getter is a free function (per §5.2), the lowering invokes the getter, not a direct member access.
  • List pattern (arr is [first, .., last]) → emit arr.Count check + indexer calls; slice/range patterns use Span-style API. On a sim-path TU, list patterns over an unordered collection (HashSet) emit XIL2CPP041 like foreach.
  • Relational pattern (obj is > 0) → obj > 0 comparison.
  • Logical patterns (and/or/not) → short-circuit &&/||/! over sub-patterns.
  • Discard _ → no emit (just true).
  • Var pattern (obj is var x) → assign to a new local + always-true.
  • default pattern [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-04] → constant pattern against the type's default; expr is default emits expr == T{} for value types or expr == nullptr for ref types.

Switch expression emit [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-07]. A switch expression var s = e switch { ... }; lowers to a _switch_result local + cascade of if statements that assign to it:

// C# input:
//   var s = e switch { 0 => "zero", < 0 => "negative", _ => "other" };

// C++ output:
::XCSharpString _switch_result;
do {
    if (e == 0) { _switch_result = ::XCSharpString{&_String_zero}; break; }
    if (e < 0) { _switch_result = ::XCSharpString{&_String_negative}; break; }
    _switch_result = ::XCSharpString{&_String_other};
} while (0);
auto s = _switch_result;

Switch statement on integer types emits as C++ switch (e) { case k: ...; break; ... }. Switch on string uses pointer-equality on the interned const FString* per §5.5: each case "label": emits as case (uintptr_t)(&_String_label): after the value s.m_storage is cast to uintptr_t. This is faster than content-compare and matches the interning invariant. case null: emits case 0: (i.e., m_storage == nullptr). [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-07]

switch on patterns: lowered same as switch expression via cascade of if's (Roslyn produces a state-machine for very large pattern switches; XIL2CPP follows the same logic).

5.7 Container and tuple emit

The container-type mapping table per Constraint §4.4:

C# typeC++ typeGC scaffolding
List<XActor>TArray<XPtr<XActor>>XGCRootSpan Strong
List<int>TArray<int32_t>None (pure RAII)
List<object>TArray<void*>XGCRootSpan Conservative + build warning XIL2CPP070
Dictionary<FName, XActor>TMap<FName, XPtr<XActor>>XGCRootSpan Strong (over values)
Dictionary<XActor, int>TMap<XPtr<XActor>, int32_t>XGCRootSpan Strong (over keys)
HashSet<XActor>TSet<XPtr<XActor>>XGCRootSpan Strong; foreach warning on sim-path
HashSet<int>TSet<int32_t>None; foreach warning on sim-path
int[]TArray<int32_t>None
XActor[]TArray<XPtr<XActor>>XGCRootSpan Strong
Span<int>TSpan<int32_t> (value-type {T*, size_t})None
Span<XActor>BANNED in MVP (XIL2CPP080)N/A — Span over XObject* requires care; deferred to post-MVP

Container emission template (List<XActor> example) [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-6 / FIX-A-MED-17 forward-commit; Rev 3 clarification per FIX-D-CRIT-04]. The Rev 1 emit used this as the parent_obj in the write barrier — this is wrong: the card-table mark should be on the XObject that owns the TArray (the field-owner), not on the TArray instance itself. The Rev 2 design adds a m_parent XObject* field to TArray.

[Rev 3: applied FIX-D-CRIT-04 — emit-timing clarification.] The m_parent field is NOT present in current XCore-4a Rev 3 TArray. It is a forward-commit to XCoreXObject Rev 5 (per XCXO-Rev5-CONTAINER-PARENT in §14.0). The container layout is layout-frozen mid-hot-reload session per Contract Rev 13.9 + the container = true flag (§8.3); adding the field is itself a layout change that requires XCoreXObject Rev 5 to coordinate (the field appends at offset 16 of TArray; existing TArray clients link against the smaller pre-Rev-5 layout). The pseudocode below shows the target representation post-XCoreXObject-Rev-5; XIL2CPP MVP cannot emit TArray instances with this layout until XCoreXObject Rev 5 ships the field.

Constructor-argument synthesis rule. For every C# expression new List<XActor>() (or any other container-of-XPtr declaration), Pass 6 emits the C++ TArray constructor with an explicit parent argument:

  • Field-storage case: class Foo : XObject { List<XActor> m_list = new(); } → the C++ field-init emits m_list(this) (parent is the enclosing XObject, supplied by C#'s field-initializer-runs-in-ctor semantics; XIL2CPP threads this through the field-init lowering).
  • Local-storage case: void M(XActor a) { var list = new List<XActor>(); list.Add(a); } → the C++ emit declares the TArray with a per-call stack-resident sentinel: ::XCore::Container::TArray<::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>> list{::XCore::Reflect::GetStackLocalSentinel()}. The sentinel is a global const XObject* that the card-table mark recognizes as the "stack-local-container" marker (per XCoreXObject Rev 5 contract). The collector handles stack-local-sentinel-marked containers via shadow-stack tracking, not card-table marking.
  • Static-field-storage case: static List<XActor> AllActors = new(); → the parent is a per-module const XObject* static-field anchor that XIL2CPP emits at the top of every .cs.cpp containing static XObject-referencing fields. The anchor is registered with XGCRoot::AddRoot at $cctor per §5.4 / FIX-A-HIGH-6.
  • Reflection-driven construction: XHT-emitted FProperty descriptors carry the parent-field offset so the runtime reflection-driven TArray construction path (Initializer Setter etc.) supplies the correct parent automatically.

The emit rule is documented in the cross-language binding table (per §10.5) so XHT's matching FProperty descriptor emit and XIL2CPP's container construction stay synchronized.

Fallback if XCoreXObject Rev 5 misses XCXO-Rev5-CONTAINER-PARENT: If Rev 5 ships without the m_parent field on TArray, the write barrier on container Add falls back to the pre-Rev-5 behavior (XPACT_GC_STORE(this, &m_data[m_count], item.Raw()) using the TArray instance as parent_obj). This is incorrect for the card-table mark (the mark is on the TArray rather than the owning XObject), but it is not catastrophic: the next mark cycle conservatively scans the TArray's slot range as a non-XObject-rooted region, picking up the new value. The cost is a per-cycle full scan of TArray contents that should be card-table-localized. Acceptable degradation pending XCoreXObject Rev 5; track via XIL2CPP171 — TArray write barrier emitting in degraded mode (XCoreXObject Rev 5 m_parent field not yet shipped) diagnostic.

The pseudocode emit (post-Rev-5; conditional on XCXO-Rev5-CONTAINER-PARENT landing):

// Closed instantiation TArray<XPtr<XActor>> emit (Rev 2):
template <>
class ::XCore::Container::TArray<::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>> {
public:
    ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>* m_data = nullptr;
    int32_t                            m_count = 0;
    int32_t                            m_capacity = 0;
    ::XCore::Reflect::XObject*         m_parent = nullptr;  // [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-6]
    ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan      m_rootSpan;
    // copy-ctor = delete;  copy-assign = delete;  (move-only per Contract §3.4)

    explicit TArray(::XCore::Reflect::XObject* parent) : m_parent(parent) {
        m_rootSpan = ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan{
            /* base   */ reinterpret_cast<void**>(m_data),
            /* stride */ sizeof(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>),
            /* count  */ 0,
            /* kind   */ ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootKind::Strong,
            /* _padding */ 0
        };
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_RegisterRootSpan(&m_rootSpan);
    }
    ~TArray() {
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UnregisterRootSpan(&m_rootSpan);
    }

    void Add(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor> item) {
        if (m_count == m_capacity) GrowAndUpdateRootSpan();
        // [Rev 2 fix: parent is m_parent (the owning XObject), not 'this' (the TArray instance)]
        XPACT_GC_STORE(m_parent, &m_data[m_count], item.Raw());
        m_count++;
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UpdateRootSpan(&m_rootSpan,
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(m_data), m_count);
    }

    // [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-19] Sort surface
    void Sort();  // requires T : IComparable<T> (enforced at Pass 3)
    void Sort(::XCore::Container::IComparer<T> comparer);

    // [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-HIGH-4] Deterministic iteration helpers
    ::XCore::Container::TArray<T> SortedKeys() const;
    ::XCore::Container::TArray<T> SortedValues() const;

private:
    void GrowAndUpdateRootSpan() {
        int32_t newCap = (m_capacity == 0) ? 4 : m_capacity * 2;
        // [Rev 2: FIX-DEFERRED-Rev3 / FIX-A-MED-6 — Realloc safety against concurrent
        //  GC marker requires XCoreXObject Rev 5 mark-phase snapshot mechanism; the
        //  current realloc may race with the marker reading old m_data slots. Coordination
        //  with XCoreXObject Rev 5 required for the snapshot protocol.]
        auto* newBuf = static_cast<::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>*>(
            ::FMemory::Realloc(m_data, newCap * sizeof(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>)));
        m_data = newBuf;
        m_capacity = newCap;
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UpdateRootSpan(&m_rootSpan,
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(m_data), m_count);
    }
};

// Use site:
//   m_inventory = TArray<XPtr<XActor>>(this);   // 'this' is the owning XObject
// (the constructor accepts the parent at construction; XHT-emitted FProperty descriptor
//  carries the parent-field offset so reflection-driven construction works correctly.)

Sim-path TMap / TSet partial specializations [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-HIGH-4]. On sim-path TUs, the TMap and TSet partial specs ship with insertion-order iteration (insertion-ordered buckets via a doubly-linked list of slots). Non-sim-path TUs may use hash-order buckets for performance. This makes foreach over a sim-path TMap/TSet deterministic across rebuilds, satisfying X-DET cross-arch determinism. TArray<K> SortedKeys() and SortedValues() are universal accessors that emit a sorted copy regardless of TU sim-path status.

TMap container emission template [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-MED-02]. The TMap closed-instantiation emit follows the same parent-XObject pattern as TArray (post-Rev-5 cascade per XCXO-Rev5-CONTAINER-PARENT):

// Closed instantiation TMap<FName, XPtr<XActor>> emit (Rev 3):
template <>
class ::XCore::Container::TMap<::FName, ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>> {
public:
    ::XCore::Container::TMapBucket<::FName, ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>>* m_buckets = nullptr;
    int32_t                                                                       m_bucketCount = 0;
    int32_t                                                                       m_count = 0;
    ::XCore::Reflect::XObject*                                                    m_parent = nullptr;
    ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan                                                 m_rootSpan;
    // copy = delete;  move-only per Contract §3.4

    explicit TMap(::XCore::Reflect::XObject* parent) : m_parent(parent) {
        m_rootSpan = ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan{
            /* base   */ reinterpret_cast<void**>(m_buckets),
            /* stride */ sizeof(::XCore::Container::TMapBucket<::FName, ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>>),
            /* count  */ 0,
            /* kind   */ ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootKind::Strong,  // (over the value column)
            /* _padding */ 0
        };
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_RegisterRootSpan(&m_rootSpan);
    }
    ~TMap() {
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UnregisterRootSpan(&m_rootSpan);
    }

    // Indexer setter (dict[key] = actor): TMap's INTERNAL barrier per §6.3 enumeration.
    void Set(::FName key, ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor> value) {
        auto* bucket = FindOrAddBucket(key);
        // [Rev 3] parent is m_parent (the owning XObject), not 'this' (the TMap instance):
        XPACT_GC_STORE(m_parent, &bucket->value, value.Raw());
        bucket->value = value;
        m_count++;  // if newly added
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UpdateRootSpan(&m_rootSpan,
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(m_buckets), m_bucketCount);
    }

    // Remove (dict.Remove(key)): same internal-barrier discipline.
    bool Remove(::FName key) {
        auto* bucket = FindBucket(key);
        if (!bucket) return false;
        // Reset to nullptr; the value's prior pointer goes through SATB:
        XPACT_GC_STORE(m_parent, &bucket->value, nullptr);
        bucket->value = ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>{};
        m_count--;
        return true;
    }

    // ... Find, ContainsKey, GetEnumerator, etc.
};

// Use site:
//   m_pickupsByName = TMap<FName, XPtr<XActor>>(this);   // 'this' is the owning XObject

TSet emit follows the same pattern with a single-column bucket (only key, no value); the XGCRootSpan covers the key column for TSet<XPtr<T>>.

Tuple literals [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-10 / FIX-B-MEDIUM-11; Rev 3 ODR resolution per FIX-D-MED-12]. Tuples emit as positional anonymous C++ structs with members Item1, Item2, ... (matching the .NET IL convention). Field names in the C# declaration (e.g., (int X, int Y)) are name-aliases that AST-normalization (Pass 2) resolves to Item1/Item2 at use sites; the C++ struct itself does NOT carry the C# field name. Two C# tuples with the same shape but different field names map to the SAME C++ struct.

ODR-safe tuple emission [Rev 3 per FIX-D-MED-12]. Every distinct tuple shape (by-position element types) emits to a SHARED inline header Intermediate/Build/<Target>/<Configuration>/Generated/Tuples.h that every .cs.cpp includes. The shared header has internal linkage via inline-keyword on the struct definition (C++17 inline variables semantic; the struct itself uses struct _Tuple_int_XCSharpString { ... }; with deduplication-safe linker treatment). This eliminates per-TU duplicate-definition risk that an in-TU emit would have. The header is emitted once per build (by the cross-module XIL2CPP closed-walk Pass 3) and consumed via include. The operator== = default defaulted comparison is required to give byte-identical results across MSVC 19.44+ / Clang 17+ / GCC 13+ — XIL2CPP's acceptance gate validates this via cross-compiler hash comparison on a known tuple corpus.

// C# input:
//   (int X, string Y) tuple = (5, "hello");
//   // tuple.X is accessible in C#; resolves at parse time to tuple.Item1.

// C++ output:
struct _Tuple_int_XCSharpString {
    int32_t Item1;
    ::XCSharpString Item2;
    // [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-11] tuple equality:
    bool operator==(const _Tuple_int_XCSharpString& other) const = default;
    bool operator!=(const _Tuple_int_XCSharpString& other) const = default;
};
_Tuple_int_XCSharpString tuple{5, ::XCSharpString{&_String_hello}};
// At use sites:
//   tuple.X → tuple.Item1  (AST-normalized at Pass 2)

// Tuple deconstruction:
//   var (a, b) = tuple;
// emits:
auto& [a, b] = tuple;   // C++ structured binding (binds to Item1, Item2 positionally)

Tuple equality semantics. C# 7.3+ (1, "a") == (2, "b") evaluates per-element. The C++ operator== = default performs per-field comparison; for tuple types containing XObject* refs, this is pointer-comparison (matching ReferenceEquals); for tuple types containing strings, this is pointer-equality on the interned const FString* (per §5.5).

5.8 Generic emit

Generic types and methods emit as C++ templates. The closed-instantiation walk at Pass 3 enumerates every reachable closed type tree; each closed instantiation emits a per-instantiation FClass with a name suffix (e.g., TArray_int, TArray_XActor) per Constraint §2.14.

The walk recursion bound is depth 16; exceeding emits XIL2CPP123 — generic instantiation closure exceeds depth 16. The walk uses a HashSet<(OpenGeneric, TypeArgs)> for cycle detection; cycles emit XIL2CPP124 — cyclic generic type closure detected.

Generic constraints [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-19 / FIX-A-MED-21 / FIX-A-MED-20 / FIX-B-MEDIUM-17 / FIX-B-MEDIUM-19 / FIX-B-MEDIUM-20]. The supported constraints:

  • where T : XObject — recipient assumes T-typed XGCRootSpan emission. XIL2CPP emits a C++ static_assert(std::is_base_of_v<::XObject, T>, "T must derive from XObject") belt-and-suspenders at the template body.
  • where T : struct — recipient may rely on value semantics. static_assert(std::is_class_v<T> || std::is_union_v<T>, ...).
  • where T : unmanaged [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-19 / FIX-B-MEDIUM-20] — recipient may rely on POD layout; emit can use direct memcpy. Important: a type containing XPtr<T> or XObject* is NOT unmanaged in the XPact sense, even though C++ would treat XPtr<T> as POD. The constraint-satisfaction analysis at Pass 3 rejects: instantiation with XObject-derived T emits XIL2CPP125 — generic constraint 'T : unmanaged' violated by closed type 'X' which is XObject-derived; instantiation with a struct containing XPtr emits XIL2CPP028 — type 'T' contains an XObject reference; cannot satisfy 'where U : unmanaged' constraint. This prevents memcpy from bypassing the write barrier.
  • where T : new() — recipient may invoke parameterless constructor. NOTE: This constraint, combined with where T : XObject, would create a Locked Commitment 3 contradiction (XIL2CPP001 bans new Foo() on XObject-derived types) — so XObject.New<T>'s constraint per Rev 2 is where T : XObject ONLY (no new(); see §2.3). For non-XObject T, the new() constraint is fine and emits as expected.
  • where T : SomeBase — recipient may upcast to SomeBase. static_assert(std::is_base_of_v<SomeBase, T>, ...).
  • where T : ISomeInterface — recipient may dispatch through interface via the FInterface table.
  • where T : notnull (C# 8+) [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-19] — emit runtime null check at the start of the method body in Dev mode; trust the analyzer in Shipping. Mirrors Roslyn's [NotNull] attribute behavior.
  • where T : default (C# 9+) [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-19] — declaratory; affects nullable-flow analysis; no emit-time effect.
  • where T : IComparable<T> / other self-referential interface [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-MEDIUM-17] — the constraint's reference to T is self-referential; resolved at closed-instantiation. The walk does NOT recurse into the constraint type for cycle detection (the constraint's T reference is "the same T we're currently instantiating").

Generic variance [Rev 2: rewrite per FIX-B-MEDIUM-18]. The Rev 1 design of "static_cast at the call site" for variance assignments is incorrect — C++ has no template variance and a static_cast between unrelated template instantiations is undefined behavior. Rev 2 implements covariance via emit-time inheritance: when IEnumerable<out T> is instantiated, the closed-instantiation walk detects variance and emits an inheritance chain (e.g., IEnumerable<Derived> inherits from IEnumerable<Base> via the FInterface table). Variance-based assignments at the C# call site (IEnumerable<Base> b = derivedEnumerable;) lower to a C++ FInterfaceImplTable* reassignment, NOT a raw pointer cast. Contravariance (in T) emits the reverse chain. If the closed-instantiation walk cannot emit the chain (e.g., the interface is open-generic in the consuming module), emit XIL2CPP026 — variance-based assignment not supported in this context; use explicit .Cast<Base>().

Cross-module generic instantiation emit owner [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-17]. A closed-generic instantiation reached from multiple consuming modules must have a single emit owner to avoid double-registration and linker collision. Rev 2 rule: the closed-instantiation FClass is emitted by the module where the closed instantiation is FIRST referenced in the dependency-aware closed-walk — specifically, the lexicographically-first dependent module that touches the closed instantiation. The closed-instantiation walk records this "principal module" in the per-action manifest; downstream consumers reference it via extern "C". The emitted symbols use inline constinit + __attribute__((selectany)) (Clang/GCC) / __declspec(selectany) (MSVC) for double-emit safety. A double-emission with conflicting principal-module attribution emits XIL2CPP129 — cross-module duplicate FClass for closed instantiation '<T>'; expected single emit by module '<M>' [Rev 4: applied FIX-F-MED-3].

Generic method FStackMapRecord location [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-HIGH-5]. Each closed instantiation of a generic method emits its own FStackMapRecord. The principal-TU rule applies: the FStackMapRecord is emitted in the lexicographically-first TU where the closed instantiation is referenced. Other TUs reference the registration via __attribute__((selectany)) / __declspec(selectany) linkage so duplicate emit is linker-safe. The collector's binary search at safe-point time sees one entry per closed instantiation, not N.

5.9 Lambda + closure + async + iterator emit

Rev 2 rewrite [applied FIX-B-HIGH-05 / FIX-B-HIGH-06 / FIX-A-MED-14]. C# lambdas capture by REFERENCE for locals, not by value. The Rev 1 "by-value capture" emit was wrong — if the captured local is modified after lambda construction, the lambda must see the new value. Roslyn implements this by hoisting captured locals into a synthesized display class at the scope-of-first-capture; the lambda becomes a member function on the display class. XIL2CPP follows Roslyn's pattern.

Lambda noexcept-correctness [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-05]. Lambda bodies are independently tier-classified at Pass 4: a lambda that may throw forces operator() to be NOT noexcept; only provably-non-throwing lambda bodies emit noexcept. The Rev 1 unconditional noexcept on every lambda was wrong — it would turn any throw into std::terminate.

Non-capturing lambda. Emits as a static C++ function pointer:

// C# input:
//   Func<int, int> square = x => x * x;
//   var y = square(5);

// C++ output (provably non-throwing → noexcept):
auto square = [](int32_t x) noexcept -> int32_t { return x * x; };
int32_t y = square(5);

Capturing lambda (display class pattern) [Rev 2 rewrite per FIX-B-HIGH-06; Rev 3 clarification per FIX-D-HIGH-03 — Roslyn-style class-with-method, NOT C++-lambda-with-capture]. At Pass 3, dataflow analysis identifies each scope's captured-locals set. Pass 6 emits one __DisplayClass_<MethodName>_<ScopeIndex> struct per scope; each captured local becomes a FIELD on the display class, and the lambda body becomes a MEMBER METHOD on the display class. References both inside and outside the lambda become field-loads through a stack-resident display-class instance. Multiple lambdas in the same scope SHARE the display-class instance (this is the load-bearing Roslyn semantic; C++-lambda-with-capture would create a distinct closure type per lambda). The Rev 2 emit was ambiguous between two lowerings; Rev 3 picks Roslyn-style explicitly.

// C# input:
//   int n = 5;
//   Func<int, int> addN = x => x + n;
//   Func<int, int> subN = x => x - n;  // shares same display class as addN
//   n = 10;            // mutation observed by both lambdas
//   int y1 = addN(7);   // y1 == 17, NOT 12
//   int y2 = subN(7);   // y2 == -3, NOT 2

// C++ output (Rev 3 — Roslyn-style display class with member methods):
struct __DisplayClass_OuterMethod_Scope0 {
    int32_t n;   // hoisted local; shared field across both lambda member methods

    // Each lambda body becomes a member method on the display class:
    int32_t __Method_addN(int32_t x) noexcept { return x + n; }
    int32_t __Method_subN(int32_t x) noexcept { return x - n; }
};
__DisplayClass_OuterMethod_Scope0 _dc0;
_dc0.n = 5;

// Delegate-typed locals bind to the member methods via a closure-pointer pair.
// addN and subN are typed as XDelegate<int32_t, int32_t> per §5.14 (post-Contract-1.1-amendment).
// MVP intermediate emit: invoke via the captured display-class pointer.
auto addN_invoke = [&_dc0](int32_t x) -> int32_t { return _dc0.__Method_addN(x); };
auto subN_invoke = [&_dc0](int32_t x) -> int32_t { return _dc0.__Method_subN(x); };

_dc0.n = 10;                  // mutates the hoisted slot; both methods observe
int32_t y1 = addN_invoke(7);   // 7 + 10 = 17
int32_t y2 = subN_invoke(7);   // 7 - 10 = -3

The thin "*_invoke" lambdas are emit intermediaries that select the appropriate member method; the canonical post-Contract delegate emit (per §5.14) replaces these with the FXDelegate<ReturnT, Args...> typed wrapper struct binding the display-class pointer as target and the member-method pointer as invoke. The MVP intermediate emit form is the one above; the canonical post-Contract emit form is wrapped in the FXDelegate type.

Why not C++-lambda-with-capture? The C++-lambda [&_dc0](...) form stores the captured display-class pointer in the closure object's hidden state. Each lambda instantiation is a distinct closure type. This conflicts with C# semantics where two lambdas in the same scope share state through the display class. The Roslyn class-with-method pattern preserves this sharing exactly; Rev 3 commits to it.

Implicit this capture [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-14]. A lambda body that references any non-static member of an enclosing class implicitly captures this. XIL2CPP captures this as a field on the display class, registers it in the XGCRootSpan (so the actor isn't collected while a lambda referencing it lives), and dispatches through this->component-style member access:

// C# input:
//   class MyActor : XObject {
//       private XComponent component;
//       public Action MakeUpdater() {
//           return () => component.Update();   // implicit 'this' capture
//       }
//   }

// C++ output (display class captures 'this' as a XPtr<XActor>):
struct __DisplayClass_MyActor_MakeUpdater_Scope0 {
    ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::MyActor> _self;
    ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan _rootSpan;

    explicit __DisplayClass_MyActor_MakeUpdater_Scope0(::MyActor* self) : _self(self) {
        _rootSpan = ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan{
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(&_self),
            sizeof(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::MyActor>),
            1,
            ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootKind::Strong,
            0
        };
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_RegisterRootSpan(&_rootSpan);
    }
    ~__DisplayClass_MyActor_MakeUpdater_Scope0() {
        // [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-06] — destructor is idempotent on move-source state.
        // If _rootSpan.base == nullptr, the span has already been unregistered by a move
        // and this destructor is a no-op.
        if (_rootSpan.base != nullptr) {
            ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UnregisterRootSpan(&_rootSpan);
        }
    }
    // Move constructor [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-4 — atomic-with-mark-phase;
    // Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-06 — invalidate move-source's _rootSpan to prevent double-unregister]
    __DisplayClass_MyActor_MakeUpdater_Scope0(__DisplayClass_MyActor_MakeUpdater_Scope0&& other) noexcept
      : _self(std::move(other._self))
    {
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UnregisterRootSpan(&other._rootSpan);
        // [Rev 3 fix] Invalidate the move-source's _rootSpan so the source's destructor
        // recognizes "already unregistered, skip" and does not double-unregister.
        other._rootSpan.base = nullptr;
        _rootSpan = ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan{
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(&_self),
            sizeof(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::MyActor>),
            1,
            ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootKind::Strong,
            0
        };
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_RegisterRootSpan(&_rootSpan);
    }
    __DisplayClass_MyActor_MakeUpdater_Scope0(const __DisplayClass_MyActor_MakeUpdater_Scope0&) = delete;
};

// The lambda body invokes member access through _self:
//   [&_dc0]() noexcept { _dc0._self.Raw()->component.Raw()->Update(); }

Capturing lambda with XObject* captures (Q11 resolution). Emits as a display class that owns an XGCRootSpan over the captured XObject* fields:

// C# input:
//   XActor owner = this;
//   Action onClick = () => owner.HandleClick();

// C++ output:
struct _Lambda_onClick {
    ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor> _captured_owner;
    ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan    _rootSpan;

    _Lambda_onClick(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor> owner)
      : _captured_owner(owner)
    {
        _rootSpan = ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan{
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(&_captured_owner),
            sizeof(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>),
            1,
            ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootKind::Strong,
            0
        };
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_RegisterRootSpan(&_rootSpan);
    }
    ~_Lambda_onClick() {
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UnregisterRootSpan(&_rootSpan);
    }
    _Lambda_onClick(const _Lambda_onClick&) = delete;
    _Lambda_onClick& operator=(const _Lambda_onClick&) = delete;
    // Move ops transfer ownership atomically per Contract §3.4

    void operator()() const noexcept {
        XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
        // Translated body of () => owner.HandleClick():
        _captured_owner.Raw()->HandleClick();
    }
};
_Lambda_onClick onClick{ ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>(this) };

Async method (non-sim-path; sim-path is banned) [Rev 2 base; Rev 3 rewrite per FIX-D-HIGH-04 / FIX-D-HIGH-07]. Per Q4 resolution: async is banned on sim-path TUs. Non-sim-path async methods emit as a state-machine struct per XCoreXObject.html §10.7.2 line 2448-2461. The Rev 3 emit shows: heap-allocation via the per-FIX-A-MED-5 path; shared-ownership capture in continuations (NOT raw [this]); a _rootSpan that covers ALL XPtr<T> locals that span awaits, not just the first.

// C# input (non-sim-path):
//   public async Task<int> LoadAssets(string path, XActor partner)
//   {
//       XActor owner = this;
//       XActor remote = partner;        // second XObject ref that spans awaits
//       byte[] data = await ReadFileAsync(path);
//       owner.OnDataLoaded(data);
//       remote.NotifyComplete();         // partner observed after the await
//       return data.Length;
//   }

// C++ output (Rev 3 — heap-allocated state machine with multi-ref _rootSpan):
struct _AsyncSM_LoadAssets {
    enum class State : int32_t { Initial, AfterReadFile, Done };
    State                                state = State::Initial;
    ::XCSharpString                      path;
    // [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-07] All XPtr<T> locals that span awaits laid out contiguously
    // so a SINGLE XGCRootSpan covers them all via start-of-locals + total-XPtr-count.
    ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>     owner;     // _liveRefs[0]
    ::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>     remote;    // _liveRefs[1] — also spans awaits
    ::XCore::Container::TArray<uint8_t>  data;       // value-typed; not in _rootSpan
    ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan        _rootSpan;  // covers owner + remote (2 slots)
    ::XCore::Promise::Promise<int32_t>   promise;

    _AsyncSM_LoadAssets(::XCSharpString p) : path(p) {
        // [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-07] _rootSpan covers the contiguous owner..remote block.
        _rootSpan = ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootSpan{
            reinterpret_cast<void**>(&owner),
            sizeof(::XCore::Reflect::XPtr<::XActor>),
            2,                                       // 2 contiguous XPtr slots: owner + remote
            ::XCore::Reflect::XGCRootKind::Strong,
            0
        };
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_RegisterRootSpan(&_rootSpan);
    }
    ~_AsyncSM_LoadAssets() {
        ::XCore::Reflect::XGC_UnregisterRootSpan(&_rootSpan);
    }

    void MoveNext() {
        // [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-04 — MoveNext takes no `self` because the state machine
        // already owns the relevant `this`-equivalent refs (owner/remote) via _rootSpan.
        // The original caller's self is captured into `owner` at Initial state.]
        XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
        switch (state) {
            case State::Initial:
                // Caller threaded `this` and `partner` into the state machine via ctor or
                // a separate init call before the first MoveNext. owner / remote already populated.
                ::XCore::Async::ReadFileAsync(path).Then(
                    // [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-04] — Shared ownership of state machine via
                    // XSharedPtr<_AsyncSM_LoadAssets> capture by VALUE (NOT [this] which would dangle
                    // on promise cancellation). The state machine itself is a heap-rooted owner;
                    // continuation lambda does NOT register a separate XGCRootSpan because the
                    // state machine's own _rootSpan already covers owner+remote.
                    [sm = ::XCore::SharedPtr::XSharedPtr<_AsyncSM_LoadAssets>(this->_shared_from_this)]
                    (auto result) {
                        sm->data = std::move(result);
                        sm->state = State::AfterReadFile;
                        sm->MoveNext();
                    }
                );
                return;
            case State::AfterReadFile:
                XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();      // per-state safe-point check
                owner.Raw()->OnDataLoaded(data);
                remote.Raw()->NotifyComplete();
                promise.SetResult(data.Num());
                state = State::Done;
                return;
            case State::Done:
                return;
        }
    }
};

Per-state safe-point checks [Rev 3]. Every state-machine state body emits a XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK() at entry so the collector can preempt the async work between awaits. The check fires per-state entry (not just per-MoveNext entry) because intermediate states may run long enough to need cooperation.

Multi-ref _rootSpan emit rule [Rev 3 per FIX-D-HIGH-07]. Every XObject reference that spans an await MUST be covered by the state machine's _rootSpan. The fields are laid out CONTIGUOUSLY in the state-machine struct so a single span (base = &first_ref, count = N) covers them all. Non-reference locals (value-typed primitives, TArray containers with own XGCRootSpan, etc.) are interleaved AFTER the reference block in the struct layout. The struct layout discipline is enforced at Pass 6 emit. If the state machine has multiple disjoint reference groups (rare; e.g., refs partitioned by which await boundary they span), emit multiple _rootSpan_0, _rootSpan_1, etc. The discipline: EVERY XPtr<T> local that crosses an await is in some _rootSpanN registered with the collector.

Async state machine allocation [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-5; Rev 3 clarification per FIX-D-HIGH-04].
