7.5 Banned-on-sim-path async

Per Q4 resolution: async / await in any function inside a sim-path TU emits diagnostic XIL2CPP044 — async/await is banned on sim-path TUs (continuation-pinning would be required; banning is the safer commitment).

7.6 Sim-path X-DET + X-NEW invariants

Per Constraint §5.5 + XCoreXObject.html §13.2:

  • X-DET (line 2840): cross-arch determinism invariant guards. 100% of sim-path TUs guarded; no false-negatives in static analysis pass.
  • X-NEW (line 2841): sim-path NewObject runtime invariant. Assert fires in Dev; abort in Shipping; no false-negatives.

7.7 Foundation Prototype criterion (c) — Cross-arch determinism

Per Constraint §5.6: same C# source → same C++ emit → bit-exact same runtime behavior on Win64 + Quest 3 ARM64 across 100 replays. Validated by the X-NEW / X-DET gates + the Sleef-vendored transcendentals + the full reproducibility envelope.

XIL2CPP's contribution: deterministic mangling, deterministic source-walk order, deterministic generic-instantiation closed-walk, deterministic Tier classification (the per-module fixpoint is order-independent and converges to a unique result), deterministic emit (sorted dict iteration, sorted file output).

8. Hot-reload Integration

8.1 Symbol mangling stability (criterion g)

Per Contract §12.2 line 2163: running XIL2CPP twice over the same input C# source produces byte-identical mangled output symbols. A method in build N+1 has the same symbol name as in build N if its signature is unchanged.

XIL2CPP's mangling rules per Contract §2.2 are deterministic by construction:

  • The mangled name depends only on (a) the contract version hash, (b) the namespace-qualified type name, (c) the method name (CLR convention), (d) the generic-arg manglings (in source-declaration order), (e) the parameter manglings (in declaration order; reference / value / by-ref / in / out prefixes).
  • The walk is deterministic: source-tree depth-first; per-tree by file path lexicographic.
  • Internal dict iteration is sorted by name before emit.

XIL2CPP's own binary is reproducible per Contract §2.4 line 526: deterministic timestamps, deterministic build IDs.

Roslyn version pinning [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-01]. Two Roslyn versions can resolve the same C# 12 source to differently-named methods in some corner cases (e.g., required member's compile-generated backing field name, primary-constructor parameter spelling, partial-class merge order). To prevent CI machine drift, Rev 2 mandates a roslyn_version field in the manifest's TargetInfo FBS schema (forward-commit XBT Rev 11 / Contract Rev 14). XIL2CPP static_asserts the manifest's roslyn_version against its built-in Roslyn version at emit time; mismatch fails the build with XIL2CPP144 — Roslyn version mismatch between manifest and XIL2CPP binary.

.NET SDK version pinning [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-04]. Similar discipline for the .NET SDK: a dotnet_sdk_version field in the manifest pins the BCL reference-assembly version. Forward-commit XBT Rev 11. Manifest mismatch emits XIL2CPP140 / 141 family.

Mangling explicitly excludes [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-01 / FIX-C-MED-02 / FIX-C-MED-03]:

  • Method body content: editing the body of a Tier 1 function MUST produce the same mangled symbol so XLiveCoding's RVA-rewrite trampoline can find the patch target.
  • Compile-time-only attributes ([Obsolete], [Conditional], [CallerMemberName], [CallerFilePath], [CallerLineNumber], [Experimental]): documentation-only changes; must not break hot-reload.
  • File path: decouples symbol identity from filesystem layout. Two developers with different repo paths produce byte-identical symbols (provided their Roslyn / .NET SDK versions match).

8.2 Vtable stability across patches

Per Contract §2.3 line 496: “no virtual-method signature changes are permitted mid-session, so the vtable layout is stable across patches.” XIL2CPP enforces this structurally:

  • The FXObjectLifecycleTable function-pointer slots are at fixed offsets (XCoreXObject §2.4); slot index is part of the cross-tool ABI envelope (the tag XPACT_XOBJECT_LIFECYCLE_TABLE_TAG pins the layout).
  • Adding a new virtual method to an XClass mid-session is rejected by XLiveCoding's Phase 1 layout-drift gate (per FIX-A-HIGH-14 in XCoreXObject Rev 4).
  • XIL2CPP emits virtual dispatchers as free functions (per §5.2); the dispatcher's address is stable across hot-reload (the symbol mangling is unchanged for unchanged signatures).

C#-source-level virtual-method table slot index protocol [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-05]. The per-FClass virtual-method table (distinct from the FXObjectLifecycleTable) holds slots for each user-author-declared virtual method. Slot indices are computed deterministically at FClass construction time:

  1. Walk the inheritance chain from the topmost XObject down.
  2. For each class in the chain, enumerate the class's directly-declared virtual methods in source-declaration order.
  3. Assign sequential indices.

The slot indices are emitted by XIL2CPP as static constexpr size_t ActorTickSlotIndex = N; in the .cs.h for the declaring class; the dispatcher (per §5.2) references this constant. A patch that adds, removes, or reorders virtual methods fails the layout-drift gate with diagnostic XIL2CPP145 — vtable slot count or order changed; class layout drift forbidden mid-session.

Generic constraint change hot-reload rejection [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-03]. Adding, removing, or modifying generic constraints (e.g., promoting void Foo<T>(T x) to void Foo<T>(T x) where T : IComparable<T>) is a SIGNATURE change for hot-reload purposes. The patch validation pass detects the change via the per-build .tier_baseline.<Module>.json sidecar's signature column and emits XIL2CPP034 — generic constraint change on existing method 'Foo<T>'; signature breakage forbidden by hot-reload commitment. Authors who need constraint changes must rebuild from scratch (no hot-patch).

Multi-interface implementation layout-freeze [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-06]. Adding or removing an interface implementation mid-session is rejected by the layout-drift gate (XIL2CPP145). The FClass's Interfaces TArray of FInterfaceImplTable* is layout-frozen: the slot ordering and count match the source-declaration order and cannot change without a full module rebuild.

8.3 Container layout freeze

Per Contract §3.7 line 737-739: container class layouts (any class with an embedded XGCRootSpan member) are layout-frozen within a hot-reload session. XIL2CPP emits container = true on every such class-descriptor.

XLiveCoding's patch-validation pass WILL compare field-offset list field-by-field [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MIN-04 — reworded as forward-looking; XLiveCoding hasn't shipped yet]; mismatch fails patch with exit codes 90-99 (Live Coding domain; codes allocated per Contract §13.1; specific code assignments TBD by XLiveCoding's spec). Allowed: function-body changes; adding new non-virtual methods. Disallowed: adding/removing/reordering data fields, relocating m_rootSpan, changing span kind, changing stride.

8.4 Sub-pool rebind cooperation (FIX-A-MIN-40) and OnClassReplaced listener discipline

Per FIX-A-MIN-40 in XCoreXObject Rev 4 §3.6: hot-reload class replacement migrates cells from the OldClass sub-pool to the NewClass sub-pool. XIL2CPP-emitted code MUST NOT assume the specific FClass*-to-cell-pool mapping survives hot-reload.

[Rev 2: REWRITE per FIX-C-CRIT-02 / FIX-C-HIGH-09] The Rev 1 design registered a raw AddRaw(lambda) handler on the OnClassReplaced delegate — this is the EXACT bug class XCoreXObject Phase 5.k introduced typed listener interfaces to prevent. When the module DLL unloads, the captured cls pointer + the lambda's .text code become invalid, the delegate retains a dangling function pointer, and the next cascade fires through the dangling pointer and crashes.

Rev 2 design: typed listener with explicit Subscribe/Unsubscribe lifecycle. XIL2CPP emits a per-module static listener object implementing the IXObjectClassReplacedListener interface (forward-commit XCoreXObject Rev 5 to publish this interface, analogous to IXObjectCreateListener per XCoreXObject §10.13). The listener registers via the canonical accessor ::XCore::CoreDelegates::GetOnClassReplaced().Subscribe(listener) returning FHandle; the listener deregisters via the matching Unsubscribe(handle) at module-shutdown time.

namespace Simgenics::XPact::Game {

// Per-module static listener — emitted by XIL2CPP at the per-module
// .init.cs.cpp aggregator, NOT per-Z_Construct_FClass site.
class XHealthPickup_OnClassReplacedListener : public ::XCore::Reflect::IXObjectClassReplacedListener {
public:
    void OnClassReplaced(const ::XCore::Reflect::FClassReplacementContext& ctx) override {
        if (ctx.OldClass == &XHealthPickup_Class) {
            // Invalidate any cached FClass* in this TU's scope:
            //  (a) Non-generic XClass static const FClass IS .rodata-resident and IS NOT replaced;
            //      but generic-instantiation FClass instances that reference XHealthPickup
            //      as a type argument DO need their internal pointers rebound via std::atomic_ref.
            //  (b) Static-field containers parameterized over XHealthPickup register their own
            //      FClass cache invalidators here (see FIX-C-HIGH-07).
            //  (c) Per-emit-site FClass caches in this TU are explicit data structures the listener
            //      walks. The cache structure is emitted at every site that caches FClass*.
            //
            // The cache walk is per-site; the listener iterates the per-module cache table.
            ::XCore::Reflect::XIL2CPP_RebindClassCache(ctx.OldClass, ctx.NewClass);
        }
    }
};

namespace {
    // [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-08] — collapse the three namespace-scope statics into
    // a single struct that enforces construction order via member-init-list. Static-init
    // ordering inside an anonymous namespace is implementation-defined; the bundled-struct
    // pattern makes the order EXPLICIT (listener is constructed before its handle is captured).
    struct ModuleInit {
        XHealthPickup_OnClassReplacedListener listener;
        ::XCore::Reflect::FHandle             handle;

        ModuleInit() noexcept
          : listener{}
          , handle{ ::XCore::CoreDelegates::GetOnClassReplaced().Subscribe(&listener) }
        {
            // listener is fully constructed before Subscribe runs because member-init-list
            // order matches declaration order (C++ standard guarantee).
        }
        ~ModuleInit() noexcept {
            // Module-unload hook: deregister BEFORE the DLL's .text segment is unloaded.
            ::XCore::CoreDelegates::GetOnClassReplaced().Unsubscribe(handle);
        }
    };
    static ModuleInit g_moduleInit;
}

extern "C" const ::XCore::Reflect::FClass* Z_Construct_FClass_MyModule_XHealthPickup() noexcept
{
    static const ::XCore::Reflect::FClass* sCachedClass = []() noexcept {
        // [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-MED-08] — safe-point at registration entry.
        XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK();
        const auto* cls = &XHealthPickup_Class;
        ::XCore::Reflect::XReflectionRuntime::RegisterClass(cls);
        return cls;
    }();
    return sCachedClass;
}

}  // namespace Simgenics::XPact::Game

The ModuleInitGuard's destructor fires during module-shutdown (DLL unload). XLiveCoding's quiesce protocol ensures all listeners are deregistered before module unload AND re-registered after module load.

Generic-instantiation FClass cache invalidation [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-08]. Closed-generic FClass instances ARE .rodata-resident (in the per-emit-site TU). Their internal references to other FClass instances (e.g., the Inner FProperty for List<XHealthPickup> pointing at XHealthPickup's FClass) are pointers, NOT stable across replacement. On OnClassReplaced(OldClass, NewClass), every closed-generic FClass that has OldClass somewhere in its argument tree must rewrite its internal pointers atomically. XIL2CPP emits per-closed-instantiation invalidation hooks: at module load, a per-site table is registered; the per-module listener walks every table entry that references the replaced FClass and updates pointers via std::atomic_ref<void*> overlay (matching XCoreXObject Phase 5.j's ClassPrivate rebind discipline).

Static-field-held container cache invalidation [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-07]. Static C# field declarations of containers parameterized over [XClass]-attributed types (e.g., public static List<HealthPickup> ActivePickups;) register an FClass cache invalidator with the OnClassReplaced delegate. The invalidator handle is emitted as a static initializer co-located with the field declaration; its lifetime is the module's (per the deregistration discipline above).

Static XObject reference unregistration on module unload [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-05]. Every static C# field of XObject-derived type (per §5.4) emits an unregistration hook that fires at module-shutdown: unregisters the field's XGCRootSpan (if any) and calls XGCRoot::RemoveRoot to release the reference. The XObject becomes GC-collectable on next sweep. The unregistration fires AFTER the OnClassReplaced delegate cycle completes — the module unload sequence is: (1) drain OnClassReplaced listeners; (2) unregister static-field roots; (3) physically unload DLL.

[XOnClassReplaced] attribute auto-registration [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-06]. Authors can mark a static or instance method [XOnClassReplaced] and XIL2CPP auto-registers it as a callback on the OnClassReplaced delegate, scoped to the declaring class. Signature MUST be void M(const FClassReplacementContext& ctx) or compile-time error XIL2CPP033 — [XOnClassReplaced] method '<M>' has wrong signature; expected void M(const FClassReplacementContext& ctx). The registration uses the per-module typed listener (above) that owns lifetime; the user-author's method becomes one of N callbacks the listener dispatches. Auto-deregister on module unload is handled by the per-module listener's destructor.

8.5 RVA-rewrite trampoline targeting

Per Contract §2.3 line 496: the free-function form with explicit self lets XLiveCoding's RVA-rewrite trampoline patch one jump at the function's entry point without vtable-slot rewriting.

Tier 1 shim functions (the extern "C" entry points with the _Shim suffix) are RVA-rewrite-target-eligible. Tier 2 direct functions are NOT individually hot-reloadable (per Contract line 974); they rebuild with their owning module.

Per-function linker section [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-18]. For single-method hot-patch < 30 s (criterion (d)), each transpiled function declares a .unique_section linker pragma so the linker places it in its own RVA range. MSVC: #pragma section(".text$xil2cpp_<StableId>", read, execute); Clang/GCC: __attribute__((section(".text.xil2cpp_<StableId>"))) + -Wl,--gc-sections. Per-function `.obj` granularity lets XLiveCoding's RVA-rewrite trampoline patch one jump at the function's entry point without re-linking the whole `.obj`.

Per-module Live-Coding manifest [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-19]. XIL2CPP emits a per-module LiveCoding.manifest.json listing every Tier 1 symbol the module exports, with: {symbol: string, rvaPlaceholder: string, sourceFile: string, stableId: string}. XLiveCoding loads this manifest at build time and on each patch attempt to map source-author intent to the patched DLL's symbol table. Published to Intermediate/Build/<Target>/<Configuration>/<Module>/Transpiled/LiveCoding.manifest.json.

8.6 Foundation Prototype criteria (d) and (e)

  • (d) Single-method hot-patch < 30 s per Contract §12.19 line 2305: validates XIL2CPP symbol mangling stability + XLiveCoding's patch-DLL discovery.
  • (e) Cascade hot-patch < 120 s nominal / < 150 s timeout per Contract line 2306: validates XIL2CPP container layout-freeze + sub-pool rebind cooperation + XLiveCoding cascade orchestration.

9. XBT Build Pipeline Integration

9.1 Action-graph placement

XIL2CPP runs as a per-module action with type XIL2CPPAction (XActionType ordinal 4 per XBT.html §5.1 line 765; Contract §10.3 line 2039). Properties [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-14]:

  • One per module containing C# sources (per XBT.html §10.5 line 1492).
  • Same-module peer with XHTAction: XHTAction(M) and XIL2CPPAction(M) run concurrent for the same M (per XHT.html §17.2 + Contract §10.1). Neither is prerequisite to the other for M.
  • Cross-module dependency: XIL2CPPAction(M) must have all of M's transitive XHT outputs available at start. Prerequisites:
    • WriteManifestAction (shared with all actions).
    • EmitReflectionAction(M_dep) for every M_dep in M's module_dependencies — produces the dependency module's .gen.manifest + reflection metadata XIL2CPP(M) consumes for cross-module NoThrow / FProperty / FClass lookup.
    • (NEW) ReferenceCompileCSharpAction(M_dep) for every M_dep with C# sources (per §9.8) — produces M_dep.refonly.dll for Roslyn type resolution. Forward-commit XBT Rev 11 amendment per FIX-C-CRIT-04.
  • Prerequisite to CompileCppAction for the module's transpiled .cpps (Contract line 2040).
  • Pass 2 mode: Tier2WholeProgramPass (XActionType ordinal 13) is a post-MVP optional action that consumes every per-module XIL2CPPAction's TierTable.partial.json and re-invokes XIL2CPP in -WholeProgramTier2 mode per module.

9.2 Invocation form

Per XBT.html §10.1 line 1472-1474:

XIL2CPP.exe -Manifest=<JSON> -ManifestBin=<FBS> -Module=<Name> [mode-options]

Mode options:
  (default; Pass 1)         no flag
  -WholeProgramTier2        with -MergedTierTable=<JSON>

In-process entry-point:
  IXIL2CPPInProcess.TranspileModuleAsync(manifest, manifestBin, moduleName, mode,
                                        mergedTierTable, cancellation);

9.3 Per-action inputs / outputs

Inputs. Per XBT.html §10.5 line 1492 + Contract §10.3 line 2039:

  • The JSON manifest + binary FlatBuffers sidecar.
  • Every .cs file in the module's csharp_sources.
  • The module's XHT-emitted reflection metadata (the .gen.manifest per XHT.html §17.3 line 1582 + the cross-language type-pair manifest).
  • Every dependency module's XHT-emitted reflection metadata (for cross-module FProperty / FClass / NoThrow annotation lookup).
  • XCoreXObject's compiled header surface (for GC ABI emit).
  • The per-target settings struct (Contract §4.5 line 885: SimPathConservativeRootsAllowed, FipsMode, contract version, platform identifier, SimdLevel default).
  • (In Pass 2 mode only) the merged TierTable.json from Tier2WholeProgramPass.

Outputs.

  • One .cs.cpp + one .cs.h per input .cs file in Intermediate/Build/<Target>/<Configuration>/<Module>/Transpiled/.
  • Per-module Transpilation.manifest.json listing every input + every output + the per-action cache key.
  • TierTable.partial.<Module>.json (Pass 1 mode only).

9.4 Cache key (per-action incremental)

Per Constraint §7.4 + XBT.html §15.1 line 1687: the per-action cache key hashes the inputs enumerated in §3.4. Cache hit produces byte-identical outputs.

9.5 In-process mode (Phase 2 perf option)

Per Constraint §7.10 + Q27 resolution: XBT may invoke XIL2CPP in-process via IXIL2CPPInProcess.TranspileModuleAsync (signature in §3.5). The cancellation token is honored at every Pass boundary.

9.6 Exit codes

Per Contract §13.1 line 2326-2349:

  • 0: success.
  • 61: XIL2CPP subprocess failure (XBT aggregator code for child-process failure).
  • 63: XIL2CPP analysis or emit failure (canonical; XBT translates child exit to 61 for aggregation).
  • 41: SimPath banned-API check failure.
  • 30: RulesAssembly compile failure.

[Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-04 / FIX-C-HIGH-12; Rev 3: applied FIX-D-CRIT-06 — reconciled with XBT run-xil2cpp stub mode.]

Two-level stub reconciliation [Rev 3 per FIX-D-CRIT-06]. There are two Phase 1 stub surfaces for XIL2CPP that must agree:

  1. XBT's run-xil2cpp mode stub (in RunXIL2CPPMode.cs) returns exit code 24 (PluginNotFound mnemonic) with a Phase 2 schedule diagnostic, symmetric to run-xht per XBT.html Rev 10 §1.1 line 258. This is the user-facing CLI mode.
  2. XIL2CPP's own binary Phase 1 stub (per FIX-C-HIGH-04) returns exit code 61 with a Phase-1-stub marker in stderr. This is the spawned-subprocess level.

The Rev 2 reconciliation correctly identified that XIL2CPP-binary returning 24 directly would collide with the PluginNotFound semantic at the action-graph level, but did not reconcile the XBT-side wrapper. Rev 3 establishes the canonical propagation: XIL2CPP binary stub returns 61; XBT's run-xil2cpp mode wraps the spawn, recognizes the 61 + Phase-1-stub marker, and translates to 24 at the CLI surface. The wrapping preserves the symmetry with run-xht (which also returns 24) and avoids the semantic collision at the action-graph level (61 is XIL2CPP-domain; 24 is plugin-domain).

Exit code summary by call surface:

  • 0: success.
  • 24: emitted by xbt run-xil2cpp CLI mode when wrapping a Phase-1-stub return. NOT emitted by XIL2CPP binary directly; only by XBT's wrapper mode.
  • 61: XIL2CPP subprocess failure (XBT aggregator code for child-process failure) OR Phase-1-stub marker on stderr (XBT's wrapper translates to 24).
  • 63: XIL2CPP analysis or emit failure (canonical XIL2CPP-internal failure). XBT's aggregator does NOT translate 63 to 24 — 63 is a real failure, not a stub indicator.
  • 41: SimPath banned-API check failure.
  • 30: RulesAssembly compile failure.

Phase-1-stub marker discipline. The XIL2CPP binary stub returns 61 with the line XPact.XIL2CPP.Phase1Stub: full implementation not yet shipped printed to stderr as the first stderr line. XBT's RunXIL2CPPMode reads the first stderr line: if it matches the marker, the mode translates the exit to 24 and emits its own Phase 2 schedule diagnostic; otherwise, the mode propagates 61 unchanged (it's a real failure). The marker discipline is part of the Phase 1 contract; the marker text is a versioned literal so a future XIL2CPP stub upgrade can change the marker without alias-breaking older XBT.

Forward-commit: Contract Rev 14 should introduce a dedicated Phase1StubReturn exit code (proposed code 90 in the reserved 90-99 Live-Coding-domain band, or a new 100+ band) to eliminate the marker-string parsing and make the stub status structurally encoded in the exit code rather than the stderr string. Until Contract Rev 14, the marker-string approach is canonical. Fallback if Contract Rev 14 misses Phase1StubReturn: the marker-string approach continues to be canonical indefinitely; no MVP critical-path block.

9.7 ABI envelope pinning

Per Constraint §3.12 + Contract §10.2 line 1821: every XIL2CPP-emitted .cs.cpp emits compile-time static_asserts pinning the manifest's ABI envelope tags to the runtime macros in XReflectionRuntime.h. [Rev 2: full enumeration per Contract Rev 13.9 §14.1-14.2 + applied FIX-C-MED-04 + FIX-A-CRIT-1 / FIX-C-CRIT-01 (verbatim Rev 13.9 content strings); Rev 3: applied FIX-D-HIGH-11 (FClass-v6 prefix added) + FIX-D-HIGH-12 + FIX-E-CRIT-2 (header count reconciled to actual emit).]

Canonical counts (Rev 3 per FIX-E-CRIT-2 reconciliation). The emitted pin block is exactly: 3 Stage-A envelope tags (gc_root_abi, exception_abi, mangling_scheme) + 24 Stage-B / Rev 13.9 layout tags (15 Stage-B reflection-type tags + 9 XObject-side tags per Contract Rev 13.9 §14.1) + 22 sizeof pins (14 reflection-type + 7 XObject-side + 1 GC-root for XGCRootSpan per Contract Rev 13.9 §14.2) + 1 alignment constraint (FStackMapRecord % 8 == 0). Total = 50 static_assert lines. The XIL2CPP-side emit is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the Contract Rev 13.9 surface; no XIL2CPP-private extensions remain after the Rev 3 reconciliation (XGCRootSpan was added to Contract Rev 13.9 §14.2 explicitly; FStackMapRecord's modulus is an alignment guard rather than a sizeof pin and is documented separately).

// At the top of every .cs.cpp:
// Stage-A envelope (3 entries; carry-forward from earlier revisions):
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_GC_ROOT_ABI_TAG,        "Span-based v1"));
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_EXCEPTION_ABI_TAG,      "Tier1-Shim/Tier2-Direct"));
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_MANGLING_SCHEME_TAG,    "Itanium-LengthPrefixed-v1"));

// Stage-B reflection-type layout tags (15 entries; verbatim Contract Rev 13.9 §14.1 content):
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_FNAME_LAYOUT_TAG,
    "FName-v1: 4+4 / Index+SerialNumber / 8-byte total / 4-byte aligned"));
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_FFIELD_LAYOUT_TAG,
    "FField-v1: 32 bytes; ClassPrivate@0, Owner@8, Next@16, NamePrivate@24"));
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_FFIELDCLASS_LAYOUT_TAG,
    "FFieldClass-v1: 48 bytes; Name@0, Id@8, CastFlags@16, SuperClass@24, Construct@32, FakeVTable@40"));
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_FFIELDVARIANT_LAYOUT_TAG,
    "FFieldVariant-v1: 8 bytes; Storage@0 (1-bit LSB tag, 0=FField, 1=FStruct, on 8-byte-aligned pointer)"));
static_assert(::XPactDetail::CompileTimeStrEq(XPACT_FPROPERTY_LAYOUT_TAG,

// Alignment constraint (NOT a sizeof pin; separate discipline; per FStackMapRecord variable-length tail):
static_assert(sizeof(::XCore::Reflect::FStackMapRecord) % 8 == 0);

A runtime rebuilt against a different ABI fails the compile loudly.

Manifest schema_version rejection [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-13]. The FBS manifest schema carries a top-level schema_version: uint32 field (forward-commit XBT Rev 11). XIL2CPP compares the manifest's schema_version against its supported set (a const list in XIL2CPP's binary). A mismatch emits XIL2CPP141 — Manifest schema version V is older than XIL2CPP supports (minimum: W); rebuild XBT with the matching schema; build exit 41.

Supported-set per ABI tag [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-10]. When a future Contract revision rolls (e.g., adds a v2 for an existing tag), XIL2CPP must know which tag-content values it supports. Rev 2 publishes the supported-set:

  • gc_root_abi supported set: {"Span-based v1"}.
  • exception_abi supported set: {"Tier1-Shim/Tier2-Direct"}.
  • mangling_scheme supported set: {"Itanium-LengthPrefixed-v1"}.
  • Reflection-type layout tags (15 entries): supported set is the verbatim Contract Rev 13.9 §14.1 content. Future Contract revisions add to the supported-set without removing prior entries (forward-compatible).
  • XObject-side layout tags (9 entries): same Rev 13.9-locked content.

A manifest declaring an unsupported tag emits XIL2CPP140 — Manifest declares unknown ABI envelope tag value '<tag>=<value>'; XIL2CPP supported: <list>; build exit 41.

Stage A vs Stage B FProperty descriptor [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-11]. XIL2CPP Rev 2 is authored against Stage B (Contract Rev 13.9). The schema-version field of the FProperty descriptor it consumes is implicitly fixed at the Rev 13.9 value. A XHT-emitted FProperty descriptor with a different schema version emits XIL2CPP146 — XHT-emitted FProperty descriptor schema version V mismatches XIL2CPP's supported value.

9.8 Reference DLL pipeline (ReferenceCompileCSharpAction)

XIL2CPP needs to resolve types across modules. The conventional approach (full source compilation per-dependency) is rejected because it makes per-module XIL2CPP serially dependent on every dependency.

Rev 3 specifies a new XBT action: ReferenceCompileCSharpAction [Rev 3: applied FIX-D-CRIT-01 / FIX-D-HIGH-01 — ordinal RE-relocated; the Rev 2 slot-17 choice violated the XBT slot-stability invariant]. Per XBT.html Rev 10 §5.1 line 788-790: "slot 17 (formerly RunPostBuildAction) is RETIRED, not reused. Slot numbers are stable; reusing 17 would alias old ActionHistory entries to a different action type." Slot 17 cannot be re-used — doing so would alias old ActionHistory.bin records to a different action type and break incremental rebuild deterministically.

Per Prime Directive, Rev 3 picks the architecturally correct option: slot 14 (Reserved_Phase2_F). Reserved slots 9-15 exist precisely so Phase 2 action types can land without rotating the CommandVersion hash — that is documented as the reservation's purpose in XActionType.cs remarks ("Pre-allocating slots 9-15 means Phase 2 work can introduce these action types without forcing a full rebuild of every existing target"). Slot 14 is the lowest-numbered free reserved slot; slot 15 (Reserved_Phase2_G) stays reserved for the next Phase 2 system. Rev 3 forward-commits XBT Rev 11 amendment:

  1. Rename XActionType.Reserved_Phase2_F = 14 to XActionType.ReferenceCompileCSharpAction = 14 in XActionType.cs (the ordinal stays; only the slot's reserved-handle name changes).
  2. Add "ReferenceCompileCSharpAction" to ContractSurface.ActionTypes (declaration order; canonicalization picks up the rename so the structure hash rotates appropriately).
  3. Document the prerequisite chain in XBT §5.1: ReferenceCompileCSharpAction(M_dep) must complete BEFORE XIL2CPPAction(M) for any M that depends on M_dep.
  4. Add .refonly.dll bytes to XIL2CPPAction's cache key (already in §3.4's Rev 2 update).
  5. Update XBT.html §5.1 commentary to retire the "slot 14 reserved for Phase 2 system F (TBD)" line and replace with the ReferenceCompileCSharpAction allocation.

Why not append a new slot 18? Adding a new highest-numbered slot is technically append-only-safe, but it rotates CommandVersion for all targets on rebuild (because the enum's value count changes, and ActionHistory.CurrentVersion is auto-derived from the enum's named values). Using one of the pre-allocated reserved slots avoids the rebuild — that is exactly why the slots exist. The reserved-slot mechanism is the documented allocation path; appending a new slot is the fallback when no reserved slot is suitable.

Fallback if XBT Rev 11 misses the slot-14 rename: XIL2CPP MVP cannot ship without the cross-module type-resolution surface. If XBT Rev 11 misses the rename, XIL2CPP hard-fails at XIL2CPPAction dispatch with XIL2CPP170 — ReferenceCompileCSharpAction not registered in XBT action graph; XBT Rev 11 amendment missing, exit code 63. There is no graceful degradation path: without reference DLLs, Roslyn cross-module type resolution cannot proceed. Authors must hold the MVP critical-path until XBT Rev 11 lands.

For each module M with C# sources:

  1. XBT runs ReferenceCompileCSharpAction(M) which compiles M's .cs files to a reference-only DLL (M.refonly.dll) using Roslyn's /refonly flag. The reference DLL contains type signatures + method signatures but no method bodies.
  2. The reference DLL is published to Intermediate/.../<Module>/Reference/M.refonly.dll.
  3. XIL2CPP(M') for any module M' depending on M loads M.refonly.dll as a MetadataReference. Type resolution + semantic analysis works against the reference DLL without parsing M's source.

The reference-DLL action is fast (~1 sec per 1k LoC) and runs concurrent with XIL2CPP(M) for each M.

Alternative path (Phase 1 simplicity): Defer ReferenceCompileCSharpAction to post-MVP and have Phase 1 MVP parse dependency module .cs files directly (lazy parse, with caching). Per Prime Directive: the integrated solution (ReferenceCompileCSharpAction) is correct; lazy parse is the easier-to-implement-but-fragile fallback. Rev 3 forward-commits the slot-14 (Reserved_Phase2_F) amendment as the chosen path per FIX-D-CRIT-01.

9.9 Reproducibility flag propagation

Per XBT.html §10.3 line 1484: XBT forwards platform reproducibility flags to XIL2CPP via the manifest. XIL2CPP propagates these to the generated C++ headers (e.g., predicating #line directives so cross-machine line-number consistency holds).

XIL2CPP's own binary is built with the reproducibility envelope (Contract §2.4 line 526). Built deterministic timestamps; deterministic build IDs; deterministic obj-file ordering.

10. XHT Reflection Consumption

10.1 The XHT / XIL2CPP boundary

Per XHT.html §17.2 line 1565-1574: XHT and XIL2CPP run concurrent against the same module. The boundary is:

  • XHT parses the module's .cs files for [XClass] / [XStruct] / [XEnum] / [XInterface] attributes; emits the C++-side .gen.h (declarations) + .gen.cpp (FClass / FStruct / FEnum / FInterface descriptor static-initialization).
  • XIL2CPP parses the same module's .cs files for bodies; emits .cs.h (per-class declarations matching the C++ structure XHT declared) + .cs.cpp (per-method bodies + lifecycle-slot bodies + ClassConstructorFn).
  • The two converge at the C++ compile step: .cs.cpp #includes .gen.h to pick up property accessors; the linker resolves Z_Construct_FClass_* symbol references between .gen.cpp (declaration of extern) and .cs.cpp (definition).

10.2 XHT-emitted metadata XIL2CPP reads

Per Constraint §8.2:

  • FClass descriptors for cross-module classes (XHT publishes via .gen.cpp; XIL2CPP reads via standard C++ include of .gen.h).
  • FProperty descriptors for each [XProperty]-attributed field.
  • NoThrow annotation bit on function descriptors (XHT emits; XIL2CPP reads at Pass 4 tier classification).
  • FXObjectRefSchemaOp opcode arrays (XHT emits per §10.4 below).
  • FXObjectLifecycleTable for C++-declared classes (XHT emits; XIL2CPP emits the matching table for C#-declared classes).

10.3 Cross-language type-pair manifest

Per XHT.html §17.3 line 1580: for each cross-language type pair (a C++ class + a C# partial class declaring the same type), the .gen.manifest's [Types] section emits both source files. XIL2CPP reads this at transpile time so the IL2CPP-emitted body code can target the same singleton-getter the C++ side references.

10.4 Q14 resolution: schema-vector emit ownership

Constraint Q14 surfaces a contradiction. XCoreXObject.html §7.4.1 line 1773 says: “XHT's emit pass walks FClass.ObjectRefProperties at compile time and emits one FXObjectRefSchemaOp per reference-carrying FProperty.” But Contract §10.2 line 1818 says: “XIL2CPP emits the matching definition in its transpiled C++ output” for C#-declared types' singleton-getters. Does the schema-vector .rodata array live with XHT-emitted .gen.cpp or XIL2CPP-emitted .cs.cpp?

Rev 1 resolution: XHT emits the schema vector for BOTH C++-declared and C#-declared classes. XIL2CPP emits only the singleton-getter function body + the FXObjectLifecycleTable slot bodies + the ClassConstructorFn.

Rationale. XHT already has the full FProperty knowledge from its [XProperty]-attribute scan of C# fields. The same scan that emits the FProperty descriptors emits the schema-vector opcodes; they're peers. Splitting this responsibility (XHT for C++; XIL2CPP for C#) would duplicate the FProperty-walking logic and create a contract surface where the two tools must produce byte-identical schema bytes for the same class. Per Prime Directive: the integrated solution is correct; the split is the easier-to-implement-but-fragile alternative.

Implication [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MIN-09]. XHT Rev 7+ must reflect this Q14 resolution. The XHT and Contract design owners are notified via the audit-trail file (this audit, this revision). The XHT Rev 7+ document must reflect:

  • XHT emits the schema vector for C# [XClass]-declared types (in addition to the existing C++ emit per XCoreXObject.html §7.4.1).
  • XHT emits the schema vector in .gen.cpp, not the C#-side .cs.cpp.
  • The cross-tool symbol contract (XToolchainContract.html §10.2 line 1818) is amended: XHT emits the FClass constinit + the schema vector; XIL2CPP emits the singleton-getter + FXObjectLifecycleTable instance + lifecycle slot bodies + ClassConstructorFn (per FIX-A-HIGH-1 reconciliation in §5.1). The FClass's LifecycleTable field is initialized to &X<Type>_LifecycleTable where the LifecycleTable instance is XIL2CPP-emitted (XHT references via extern; XIL2CPP defines).

10.5 Cross-language binding table (shared NuGet)

Per XHT.html §17.4 line 1617: both tools share the binding table via the XPact.Mangling shared NuGet. XIL2CPP at parse + emit time consults the table for: primitive mappings (bool→bool, int→int32_t, float→float, string→XCSharpString, char→char16_t [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-23]), container mappings (List<T>→TArray<T'>, etc.), XObject subclass mappings.

Canonical auto-property backing-field naming [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-HIGH-09; Rev 4: applied FIX-F-MED-3]. Both XHT and XIL2CPP MUST use __BackingField_<X> for auto-property backing fields (deterministic, C-identifier-safe; NO angle brackets). A drift between the two emits diagnostic XIL2CPP149 — backing-field naming mismatch between XHT and XIL2CPP for property '<X>'.

XPact attribute namespaces (canonical) [Rev 2: applied FIX-A-MED-32 / FIX-A-MED-1]. The C# attributes XIL2CPP recognizes live in published, well-known namespaces:

  • XPact.CoreXObject.XObjectInternalConstructorAttribute — suppresses XIL2CPP001 inside the factory.
  • XPact.CoreXObject.XFunctionAttribute — carries NoThrow, CanThrow, sim-path-suppression flags.
  • XPact.CoreXObject.XClassAttribute, XPact.CoreXObject.XStructAttribute, XPact.CoreXObject.XEnumAttribute, XPact.CoreXObject.XInterfaceAttribute — mark types for reflection emit.
  • XPact.CoreXObject.XPropertyAttribute — marks fields for reflection emit.
  • XPact.CoreXObject.XOnClassReplacedAttribute [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MED-06] — auto-registers method as OnClassReplaced callback.
  • XPact.CoreXObject.XValueClassAttribute [Rev 2: applied FIX-B-CRIT-03] — opts in to value-semantic emit for plain non-XObject classes.

10.6 What XIL2CPP does NOT consume

Per Contract §10.2 line 2029: neither parses any other tool's binary output during their own work. The manifest is the join key. XIL2CPP does NOT parse XHT's .gen.h content as data; it reads it via standard C++ include for the property accessors.

11.2 XIL2CPP-specific gates

In addition to inheriting the XCoreXObject gates above, XIL2CPP introduces its own acceptance gates:

GateDescriptionPass criterion
X-IL2CPP-MANGLE-DETMangling determinismRunning XIL2CPP twice on identical input C# source produces byte-identical .cs.cpp output, verified via SHA-256 comparison across 100+ test modules.
X-IL2CPP-TIER1-ABITier 1 shim ABI cross-configDev shim signature = Shipping shim signature for every Tier 1 function; verified via XPact's contract conformance test suite (Contract §12.3).
X-IL2CPP-TIER2-PROOFTier 2 noexcept proofEvery Tier 2-classified function has noexcept proof; [XFunction(NoThrow = true)]-attributed functions that fail proof emit XIL2CPP030 + exit 63.
X-IL2CPP-GENERIC-DEPTHGeneric closure depth boundDepth-16 walk emits XIL2CPP123; cycle emits XIL2CPP124. Synthetic test suite: 25 closed types at depth 15 (pass), 1 at depth 17 (XIL2CPP123), 1 self-recursive (XIL2CPP124).
X-IL2CPP-NEW-FACTORYLocked Commitment 3 enforcementEvery new Foo() on XObject-derived emits XIL2CPP001 + exit 63. Synthetic test: 50 test classes, each constructed via new Foo() with intentional violation; all 50 emit the diagnostic.
X-IL2CPP-SIMPATH-NEWSim-path NewObject runtime invariantEvery sim-path XObject.New<T> call emits the XPACT_CHECK_SL guard. Synthetic test: 100 sim-path calls; runtime check fires on every cross-thread invocation.
X-IL2CPP-STACKMAP-COVStack-map coverage100% of XIL2CPP-emitted functions have an FStackMapRecord in .rodata; the collector's binary search finds the record for every running function. Test: 10k functions emitted; 10k FStackMapRecord entries verified.
X-IL2CPP-BARRIER-EMITWrite barrier emission100% of reference-storing assignments emit XPACT_GC_STORE. Static-analysis test: feed XIL2CPP a synthetic C# corpus with 1000 reference writes; verify the emitted C++ contains 1000 XPACT_GC_STORE invocations.
X-IL2CPP-CACHE-HITIncremental cache correctnessRunning XIL2CPP twice on identical inputs with the cache primed produces byte-identical outputs without re-running parse/emit; cache hit rate > 99% on rebuild-after-no-change.
X-IL2CPP-CSPATH-DETCross-arch C# determinismThe same .cs source compiles to bit-exact same C++ on Win64 + Quest 3 build machines. Sub-test of (c).
X-IL2CPP-LATENCYTranspile latencyPer-module transpile latency ≤ 10 ms / 1000 LoC on Engineer-station Win64 baseline. Target: 10k LoC module in ≤ 100 ms.
X-IL2CPP-SIZEOutput sizeTranspiled .cs.cpp size ratio: ≤ 4x the input C# .cs LoC measured against the §13 Foundation Prototype corpus (SyntheticActorCorpus + SyntheticContainerCorpus). Mean ratio ≤ 4x; per-file 95th percentile ≤ 6x. [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-MIN-08]
X-IL2CPP-CASCADE-INCREMENTAL [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-HIGH-19]Cascade hot-patch incremental rebuildCascade-driven re-transpilation of 10 dependent modules of 10k LoC each completes in ≤ 60 s (leaving 60 s for compile-link). Per-function cache (per §3.4) lets unchanged functions skip re-emit. XInsights telemetry tracks function_cache_hit_rate.
X-IL2CPP-SHADOWSTACK-COV [Rev 2 addition]Shadow-stack roots coverage100% of XObject* locals captured in _liveRefs shadow-stack arrays (Rev 2 mechanism per §6.1). Test: SyntheticActorCorpus with 100 nested functions, each with 1-5 stack-resident XPtrs — the collector enumerates every live ref via the shadow-stack indices.

XIL2CPP200Runtime ErrorModule unload while OnClassReplaced listener still registered; potential dangling-pointer hazard [Rev 2: applied FIX-C-CRIT-02]§8.4

Diagnostic emission protocol. Every diagnostic:

  • Carries an exact source location (file:line:col).
  • Carries a fix-it suggestion where applicable.
  • Is non-suppressible via #pragma warning disable (the diagnostic system ignores suppression for X-prefixed codes).
  • Is emitted as a structured JSON entry in XIL2CPP.diagnostics.json at the per-module output directory (for IDE consumption).
  • Aborts the build with exit code 63 (analysis) or 41 (sim-path banned-API).

13. Foundation Prototype Mapping (Implementation Phases)

Per Master Plan §11 Step 5.5: the Foundation Prototype is the milestone where the XPact toolchain is exercised end-to-end against pass criteria (a) through (i). XIL2CPP's Foundation Prototype contribution maps to:

  • Phase 6.l (the harness phase): a synthetic gameplay corpus that exercises all XIL2CPP emit paths, runs the X-* gates listed in §11.2, exercises criteria (c), (d), (e), (f), (g) via XIL2CPP-emitted code, and reports pass/fail.

The Foundation Prototype harness consists of [Rev 2: expanded with FIX-A-CRIT-2 shadow-stack + boxing-ban coverage]:

  1. SyntheticActorCorpus: 50 C# classes, each : XObject, with 5-15 fields including XObject references, mixed XPtr and value types, 5-20 methods including virtual / override / sealed combinations, 2-5 generic instantiations.
  2. SyntheticContainerCorpus: 25 C# classes exercising every container partial specialization (List<XActor>, Dictionary<FName, XActor>, HashSet<XActor>, List<int>, Dictionary<int, int>, etc.).
  3. ShadowStackCoverageTest [Rev 2 addition per FIX-A-CRIT-2]: 100 nested transpiled functions, each with 1-5 stack-resident XPtrs — verifies the shadow-stack mechanism enumerates every live ref; uses the SyntheticActorCorpus as input.
  4. SimPathConvergenceTest: a sim-path TU that exercises 100 NewObject calls across 100 frames; X-NEW gate validates the assertion fires from a worker pool thread.
  5. BoxingBanTest [Rev 2 addition per FIX-B-CRIT-02 / FIX-B-CRIT-06; Rev 3 cleanup per FIX-D-LOW-05]: 30 sim-path TUs that attempt to box value types via object o = 5;, new { Actor = a, Count = 5 };, List<object> with value entries, etc. Every TU MUST emit the appropriate diagnostic. Validated codes: XIL2CPP060 / 061 / 062 / 073 (XIL2CPP001 removed; XIL2CPP001 belongs to X-IL2CPP-NEW-FACTORY test, NOT this test — conflating the two confuses test target coverage).
  6. HotReloadCascadeTest: 10 modules each declaring 5 classes; one base class change cascades; criterion (e) timing measured. Also exercises the per-function cache: re-transpile after a no-op edit should be ≤ 1 s.
  7. OnClassReplacedListenerTest [Rev 2 addition per FIX-C-CRIT-02]: module load/unload cycle 100 times; verify no listener-leak; verify the typed listener pattern correctly registers AND unregisters.
  8. CrossArchReplayTest: 100 replays on Win64 + Quest 3 in lockstep; bit-exact state at every frame.
  9. SymbolStabilityTest: rebuild the SyntheticActorCorpus twice; SHA-256 every output; assert byte-identical.
  10. DeterminismMathTest [Rev 2 addition per FIX-A-HIGH-3; Rev 4: applied FIX-F-MED-1]: sim-path TU calling every Math.* method enumerated in §7.8; verify cross-arch bit-exact behavior; verify Random.* / Stopwatch / locale-dependent ToString emit XIL2CPP049 / XIL2CPP040 / XIL2CPP058.

14. Open Items (Round 1 audit arbitration table)

The Round 1 audit has scrutinized the Rev 1 commitments. The Rev 2 audit table is rebuilt with three classes of items: (a) Round 1 items resolved in Rev 2; (b) cross-system Contract amendments / forward-commitments tracking; (c) new items for Round 2 audit. [Rev 2 rewrite.]

The legacy Rev 1 audit table is retained below for traceability.

14.0 Forward-commitments to other systems [Rev 3: applied FIX-E-CRIT-3 (split XException) + FIX-E-CRIT-4 (fallback column) + FIX-E-HIGH-1 through 9 + FIX-E-MED-1 through 3 + FIX-E-MED-6 + FIX-E-LOW-2 (normalize IDs) + FIX-D-MED-11 (XCSharpStringBuilder)]

The following forward-commitments require coordinated work in other XPact specs / docs (carried forward beyond Rev 3). Every row carries a documented FALLBACK per FIX-E-CRIT-4: if the receiving spec misses the requested feature in its scheduled revision, the listed behavior is what XIL2CPP MVP does instead. Forward-commit IDs follow the <TargetSpec>-Rev<N>-<FeatureName> convention per FIX-E-LOW-2.

Forward-commit IDRequired byDescription (Rev 3 fingerprint)Fallback if missedOrigin
XCXO-Rev5-SAFEPOINTXCoreXObject Rev 5Publish XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK macro defined in Engine/Source/Runtime/XCore/Public/XSafePoint.h; TLS variable ::XCore::Reflect::XGCSafepoint::s_tlsPendingFlag (1-byte per-thread; updated by fan-out broadcast); pending-handler ::XCore::Reflect::XGCSafepoint::HandlePending(); ABI tag XPACT_SAFEPOINT_FLAG_ABI_TAG in Contract surface (Rev 14 addition).HARD-FAIL at build with XIL2CPP172 — XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK macro not published by XCoreXObject Rev 5; no MVP critical path without safe-points (GC correctness depends on them).FIX-A-CRIT-6
XCXO-Rev5-GCSTORE-MEMXCoreXObject Rev 5[Rev 3: applied FIX-E-HIGH-8 per-arch fingerprint] x86-64 uses memory_order_relaxed for both SATB enqueue and slot store (TSO arch); ARM64 uses memory_order_release for both. Tag content: "XGCStore-v1: SATB enqueue (relaxed on x86, release on ARM64); slot store (relaxed on x86, release on ARM64); read-side acquires inferred from architecture". Add XPACT_GC_STORE_MEMORY_ORDER_TAG to Contract Rev 13.9 §14.1 (or Rev 14).Conservative default: emit memory_order_release on both archs. Correct but loses ~2 cycles per barrier on x86. XIL2CPP173 — GC store memory-order forward-commit not yet honored; emitting conservative cross-arch release warning at build.FIX-A-HIGH-10, FIX-E-HIGH-8
XCXO-Rev5-NEWROOTXCoreXObject Rev 5NewRootObject<T>(Name, Flags) C++ factory variant (no Outer) at Engine/Source/Runtime/XCore/Public/XObject.h; restricted to internal-engine use via [XObjectInternalConstructor].Hard-fail at any C# call site of XObject.NewRoot<T> with XIL2CPP174 — XObject.NewRoot factory not yet shipped; XCoreXObject Rev 5 missing. Root-objects can be created via legacy NewObject<T>(TransientPackage, Name, Flags) with explicit transient outer until Rev 5 ships; warn diagnostic only.FIX-A-LOW-8
XCXO-Rev5-LISTENERXCoreXObject Rev 5[Rev 3: applied FIX-E-MED-2 fingerprint extension] Publish IXObjectClassReplacedListener interface + ::XCore::CoreDelegates::GetOnClassReplaced().Subscribe(listener) returning FHandle; module-shutdown Unsubscribe discipline. Firing semantics: per-XClass replacement (not per-module); fired on the XBT-self thread after rebind but before user code resumes; re-entrant Subscribe inside the listener body allowed but deferred to the next replacement event (avoiding infinite loop).Hard-fail: Phase 6.k cannot ship without the listener interface. XIL2CPP175 — IXObjectClassReplacedListener not published; XCoreXObject Rev 5 missing; hot-reload cascade hot-patch unavailable. Criterion (e) cascade hot-patch is gated.FIX-C-CRIT-02, FIX-A-MED-33, FIX-C-HIGH-09, FIX-E-MED-2
XCXO-Rev5-CONTAINER-PARENTXCoreXObject Rev 5[Rev 3: applied FIX-E-HIGH-7 split into three sub-fingerprints] Sub-1: TArray<T>(XObject* parent) constructor signature at Engine/Source/Runtime/XCore/Public/Container/TArray.h; TArray<int> (no XObject elements) still accepts parent for API uniformity; parameterless ctor reserved for stack-local-sentinel default. Sub-2: Card-table-mark API: MarkCard(XObject* parent) per-cache-line granularity (64 bytes) at XGCWriteBarrier.h. Sub-3: Realloc mark-phase snapshot mechanism: two-phase commit — (1) acquire snapshot lock on mark phase, (2) realloc-copy under lock, (3) atomic pointer swap + release. Cost: ~3-5 µs per realloc on contended scenarios.Degraded mode per §5.7 Rev 3 fallback: use this (TArray instance) as parent_obj for write barriers; conservative GC scan on next cycle picks up the new value. Warn via XIL2CPP171 — TArray write barrier emitting in degraded mode.FIX-A-MED-6, FIX-A-MED-17, FIX-E-HIGH-7
XCXO-Rev5-LAZY-INITXCoreXObject Rev 5[Rev 3: applied FIX-E-MED-1 mechanism fingerprint] Per-module XStackMapTable wrapped in LazyInit<XStackMapTable>; population is single-threaded inside FClass-module-load, which is already serialized through XReflectionRuntime::RegisterClass. Init mechanism: function-local static (C++11 Magic Statics) for thread-safe first-call init; no double-checked locking needed.Eager-init at module load (current Rev 1 behavior); ordering hazards may surface on cross-module static-init-order edge cases but workable. XHT-Rev7-SCHEMA-VECTORXHT Rev 7[Rev 3: applied FIX-E-HIGH-1 fingerprint extension] Input: XHT consumes the per-module .cs.h declarations emitted by XIL2CPP Phase 6.e to discover C#-declared XClasses and their FProperty fields. Output: XHT emits the FXObjectRefSchema opcode vector + extern declaration in the per-class .gen.h. Ordering: XHT's action runs AFTER XIL2CPP's emit action in the action graph; XBT slot 14 (ReferenceCompileCSharpAction) is XIL2CPP prereq, but XHT's reflect action is XIL2CPP's downstream consumer for C#-declared types. Failure mode: If XIL2CPP emits a C#-declared XClass with an FProperty XHT cannot understand (e.g., unknown generic instantiation), XHT emits typed diagnostic and the build fails. [Cross-ref FIX-E-HIGH-9 opcode bit-mask MVP/post-MVP table to land in XCoreXObject §7.4.]Conservative XGCRootSpan scan for C#-declared XClasses (no schema vector emitted; collector falls back to walking the entire XObject struct word-by-word as if Conservative). 4× GC walker cost (per criterion (b) X-SCHEMA budget). Warn via XIL2CPP177 — schema vector emit not supported by XHT; conservative scan fallback active.FIX-A-HIGH-1, FIX-E-HIGH-1, FIX-E-HIGH-9, Q14 carried-forward

Third-party scope: C++ symbols in modules NOT processed by XHT (third-party SDKs) are conservatively classified as throwing by XIL2CPP Phase 2; XIL2CPP01x diagnostic if a sim-path function transitively calls a throwing C++ callee. Fingerprint: MangledSymbol uses Contract Rev 13.9 mangling scheme; the C++ symbol's NoThrow attribution is derived from its declaration site (the .h is canonical; the .cpp is informational).Conservative T1 shim for all C++ callees (correct but slow). 14.1 Resolved questions (Rev 1 honors the locked commitments)

The following questions from the constraint inventory were resolved at Rev 1 by the user-locked commitments:

  • Q1 (C# language version): RESOLVED. C# 12 (.NET 8) MVP per Locked Commitment 2 (§2.2).
  • Q3 (CLR coexistence): RESOLVED. Pure transpilation; no hosted CLR per Locked Commitment 1 (§2.1). The 58-day-old project_architecture memory entry is SUPERSEDED.
  • Q13 (new Foo() semantics): RESOLVED. new Foo() for XObject-derived is COMPILE-TIME ERROR; explicit factory XObject.New<T>(outer, name, flags) required per Locked Commitment 3 (§2.3, §5.3).
  • Q28 (IL vs C# source): RESOLVED. C# source via Roslyn, NOT IL bytecode per Locked Commitment 1.
  • Q14 (schema-vector ownership): RESOLVED in this doc. XHT emits for both C++- and C#-declared types (§10.4).
  • Q4 (async sim-path): RESOLVED. async/await BANNED on sim-path; XIL2CPP044 (§5.9, §7.5).
  • Q5 (lock semantics): RESOLVED. lock BANNED on sim-path; on non-sim-path maps to FScopedCriticalSection over per-object monitor (§5.10).
  • Q6 (unsafe blocks): RESOLVED. unsafe allowed for non-XObject types; banned on sim-path (§5.18).
  • Q7 (reflection runtime): RESOLVED. typeof / GetType supported; dynamic Type.GetMethods / MethodInfo.Invoke post-MVP (§5.11).
  • Q8 (checked/unchecked): RESOLVED. Default unchecked (C++ semantics); checked block emits explicit overflow check (§5.16).
  • Q9 (fixed statement): RESOLVED. fixed is no-op cast on non-moving GC (§5.17).
  • Q10 (stackalloc and Span): RESOLVED. stackalloc maps to alloca; Span as {T*, size_t} value type (§5.13).
  • Q11 (lambda capturing XObject*): RESOLVED. Closure struct with XGCRootSpan over captures (§5.9).
  • Q12 (yield return): RESOLVED. Same as async: state machine + XGCRootSpan (§5.9).

15. Implementation Order (Sub-Phase Breakdown)

XIL2CPP is the largest single tool in the XPact toolchain after XCoreXObject. The implementation phase (Phase 6, post-XCoreXObject Phase 5 completion) is broken into sub-phases. Each sub-phase has clear deliverables, testing gates, and a duration estimate at the foundation-tool cadence.

  1. Phase 6.a — Roslyn front-end + parse-tree consumption (Pass 1) [Rev 3: rewrite per FIX-D-HIGH-01 / FIX-D-CRIT-01]: ship the XIL2CPP CLI binary scaffold; integrate Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp v4.8+; implement the manifest reader (JSON + FBS); implement the per-module C# parse via CSharpSyntaxTree.ParseText + CSharpCompilation with reference-DLL metadata; ship the ReferenceCompileCSharpAction (XBT slot 14 per §9.8 Rev 3 ordinal allocation; the Rev 1 ordinal-14 claim was correct but for the wrong reason — Rev 1 collided with the reservation, Rev 3 occupies it explicitly). Unit tests verify parse of every C# 12 syntax feature in §4.1. Estimated: 2-3 weeks.
  2. Phase 6.b — AST normalization + semantic analysis (Pass 2 + Pass 3): ship the AST normalization pass (expand expression-bodied members, lower pattern matching, flatten using-blocks, expand collection expressions, normalize record positional ctors, expand with expressions, lower string interpolation); ship semantic analysis (XIL2CPP001 diagnostic; sim-path banned-API enumeration; reference-store enumeration; container enumeration; lambda capture analysis; async/iterator site enumeration; generic instantiation closed-walk; reflection consumption; cross-module callee NoThrow lookup). Unit tests verify each pass output against a golden corpus. Estimated: 3-4 weeks.
  3. Phase 6.c — Tier classification Pass 1 (Pass 4): ship the per-module fixpoint Tier 2 classification; ship TierTable.partial.<Module>.json emit; ship cross-module callee NoThrow lookup via XHT-emitted reflection metadata. Unit tests verify Tier 2 classification against synthetic corpus + verify Tier 1 conservatism on unresolved cross-module callees. Estimated: 1-2 weeks.
  4. Phase 6.d — Two-pass tier classification Pass 2 (post-MVP): ship the Tier2WholeProgramPass action (XActionType ordinal 13); ship the -WholeProgramTier2 XIL2CPP mode; ship the per-module re-emit deltas. Unit tests verify Tier 2 promotion never demotes; verify whole-program fixpoint converges. Estimated: 1-2 weeks. Off-MVP-critical-path; can ship after Phase 6.l.
  5. Phase 6.e — C++ emit: classes / methods / properties (Pass 5 + Pass 6 baseline): ship the mangling assignment (Pass 5); ship the basic C++ class emission (XCLASS body, ctor, FClass StaticClass() body, ClassConstructorFn, Z_PostInitProperties_*, lifecycle slot bodies, FXObjectLifecycleTable bridge); ship per-method Tier 1 shim + Tier 2 direct emit; ship the property getter/setter pair emit. Unit tests verify the emitted C++ compiles standalone; verify mangling determinism; verify ABI envelope static_asserts emit at the top of every .cs.cpp. Estimated: 4-5 weeks.
  6. Phase 6.f — C++ emit: container types + GC-aware partial specializations: ship the per-closed-instantiation container emission (TArray, TMap, TSet partial specs with XGCRootSpan registration in ctor + update on resize + unregister in dtor); ship move-only semantics; ship container = true class-descriptor flag; ship Conservative kind warning + dedup. Unit tests verify the emitted containers pass round-trip serialization + survive synthetic GC cycles + survive hot-reload class replacement. Estimated: 3-4 weeks.
  7. Phase 6.g — Stack-map emit + safe-point check + GC root span emit [Rev 3: rewrite per FIX-D-CRIT-02]: ship the per-function FStackMapRecord emission with the shadow-stack mechanism (XPtr<XObject>* _liveRefs[N] stack array per §6.1 Rev 2 rewrite per FIX-A-CRIT-2 — NOT explicit register spilling, which was rejected as architecturally infeasible under the locked toolchain matrix per §6.1); ship the XPACT_SAFEPOINT_CHECK at function entry + back-edge; ship the long-loop detection (Pass 3 augmentation); ship the XStackMapTable registration at module load via the lazy-init pattern (FIX-A-MED-30). Per-platform shadow-stack is platform-NEUTRAL by design (per FIX-C-HIGH-17; the Win64 and ARM64 layouts are identical because the array of pointers is portable C++; offsets are array-relative, not register-relative). Unit tests verify 100% function coverage via _liveRefs index tracking + verify the collector's binary search finds the right record + verify long-loop detection threshold + verify the safe-point ordering (shadow-stack init → container constructors → safe-point check → user body per FIX-D-HIGH-02 §6.1). Estimated: 3-4 weeks. X-IL2CPP-STACKMAP-COV + X-IL2CPP-SHADOWSTACK-COV + X10 acceptance gates.
  8. Phase 6.h — Write barrier insertion: ship the XPACT_GC_STORE emission at every reference-store site (per Pass 3 enumeration); ship the new-object-init barrier omission; ship the cross-property-chain emit. Unit tests verify 100% barrier coverage + verify barrier cost ≤ 5 cycles. Estimated: 2 weeks. X-IL2CPP-BARRIER-EMIT + X4 acceptance gates.
  9. Phase 6.i — async/await + lambda + iterator state-machine emit (with sim-path enforcement): ship the closure-struct emit for lambdas with XObject captures; ship the state-machine emit for async methods (non-sim-path); ship the state-machine emit for yield-return iterators; ship the XIL2CPP044 diagnostic for sim-path async. Unit tests verify XGCRootSpan registration over captures; verify state-machine MoveNext correctness; verify the async state machine survives a GC cycle mid-await. Estimated: 3-4 weeks.
  10. Phase 6.j — Exception handling emit (try/catch/finally + throw): ship the Tier 1 shim emit for functions that throw / catch; ship the Dev/Test native-exception lowering; ship the Shipping XResult lowering; ship the per-call-site XResult destructor teardown; ship XCSharpException type infrastructure. Unit tests verify cross-config ABI (Dev shim sig = Shipping shim sig); verify exception propagation through Tier 1 boundaries; verify XResult RAII destructor fires correctly. Estimated: 3 weeks. X-IL2CPP-TIER1-ABI acceptance gate.
  11. Phase 6.k — Hot-reload integration (symbol mangling stability + cross-module rebind) [Rev 3: rewrite per FIX-D-CRIT-05]: ship the typed IXObjectClassReplacedListener per-module static listener emission at the .init.cs.cpp aggregator (per §8.4 Rev 2 rewrite per FIX-C-CRIT-02 / FIX-C-HIGH-09 — NOT per-Z_Construct_FClass-site emission; per-site would register N listeners per module instead of 1, change deregistration sequencing in module-unload, and break the ModuleInitGuard correctness pattern); ship the explicit Subscribe/Unsubscribe lifecycle around ModuleInitGuard; ship the per-FClass sub-pool rebind cooperation; ship the per-closed-instantiation generic FClass cache invalidation hooks (FIX-C-HIGH-08); verify symbol mangling stability across rebuild via SHA-256 comparison; verify container layout-freeze enforcement. Integration tests with XLiveCoding (synthetic patch DLL) verify Tier 1 shim RVA rewrite + Tier 2 module rebuild path + module-unload listener-leak prevention. Estimated: 2-3 weeks. X-IL2CPP-MANGLE-DET + criterion (d) + criterion (e) acceptance gates.
  12. Phase 6.l — Foundation Prototype harness for X10, Criterion (c), Criterion (d), Criterion (e), Criterion (g): ship the SyntheticActorCorpus + SyntheticContainerCorpus + SimPathConvergenceTest + HotReloadCascadeTest + CrossArchReplayTest + SymbolStabilityTest; ship the X-* gate validation tests; integrate with XInsights telemetry for transpile-time metrics. Estimated: 3-4 weeks. All XIL2CPP-side Foundation Prototype gates.

Total estimated implementation duration: ~30-40 weeks (7-9 months) at the foundation-tool cadence (~6-8 weeks per major sub-phase). This is the largest single tool in the engine apart from XCoreXObject; the spec is long because the system is large.

Critical path. Phases 6.a → 6.b → 6.c → 6.e are sequential prerequisites; 6.f and 6.g can parallelize after 6.e; 6.h is gated by 6.g (the FStackMapRecord emit informs the write barrier emit site); 6.i + 6.j are independent and parallelize; 6.k is gated by 6.e + 6.f + 6.g; 6.l is the final integration phase.

Sub-doc split decision. Per the XCoreXObject Rev 2 pattern: this Rev 1 ships as one umbrella spec. The structural §-substructure can split at implementation-phase boundaries if the work units crystallize that way (e.g., Phase 6.g could split into a dedicated stack-map spec; Phase 6.i could split into an async-state-machine spec). Rev 1 commits to umbrella for the design phase.
